assistant: I found no `$ref` handling anywhere in `src` or `tests` (grep returns nothing). Today a mapping with a `$ref` key is an ordinary mapping. All paths below are under `/work/repo`.\n\n## 1. Module layout\n\n**`src/troupe/cli/`**\n- `main.py`: the `troupe config` parser and `run()` (l.105). It catches `TroupeError`, prints it to stderr and returns `exit_code`.\n- `shell.py`: `TroupeArgumentParser` (usage errors exit 2) and the shared `--theatre` option.\n- `get_show_command.py`: `handle_get` (l.133) and `handle_show` (l.159), including `--sources`.\n- `render.py`: `format_show_output` and `source_label(file, theatre_dir)` (l.82).\n- `yaml_output.py`: `render_yaml` (l.82), which prints block YAML with per-line `# from <file>`.\n- `set_command.py`: the `set PATH VALUE [--reason]` parser and handler.\n- `watch_command.py`, `actor.py`: the watch command and the `TROUPE_ACTOR` actor.\n\n**`src/troupe/config/`**\n- `constants.py`: `INCLUDE_PREFIX="file:"` (l.15) and `YAML_INCLUDE_SUFFIXES=(".yml",".yaml")`.\n- `errors.py`: `ExitCode`, `TroupeError` and its subclasses.\n- `model.py`: the frozen source-mapped model. `loader.py`: `load_config`. `locate.py`: `find_config_file`.\n- `paths.py`: `ConfigPath`. Only empty segments are rejected, so `$ref` is already a valid PATH segment.\n- `resolve.py`: `walk_path`, `resolve_path`, `flatten_leaves`.\n- `reconcile.py`, `recorded.py`, `history.py`, `store.py`, `lineage.py`, `lock.py`: history, the SQLite store and the lock.\n- `theatre.py`: `open_theatre`, `get_value`, `node_at`. `watch.py`: `Watcher`. `scalars.py`, `yaml_style.py`: scalar typing and quoting.\n\n**`src/troupe/config/writeback/`**\n- `target.py`: `resolve_target`. `planner.py`: `plan_patch`. `layout.py`: `FileLayout`. `edits.py`: `TextEdit` and `apply_edits`.\n- `compare.py`: plain trees and `splice`. `verify.py`: `verify_patch`. `setter.py`: `set_value`. `leaf_diff.py`: `diff_leaves`.\n- `collection_render.py`, `scalar_render.py`, `block_scalar.py`: rendering. `value.py`: the VALUE parser. `atomic.py`: the atomic writer.\n\n**`tests/`**\n- `conftest.py`: the `make_theatre` and `run_cli` fixtures.\n- `support.py`: `write_theatre` and `invoke_troupe`, which runs the real console script.\n- `set_support.py`: `run_set`, `run_config`, `run_config_in_process`, `assert_file_bytes`, `snapshot_tree`.\n- `watch_support.py`: the watch subprocess helper.\n- Test files are named per spec:\n  - foundation: `test_loader*.py`, `test_e2e_includes.py`, `test_paths_resolve.py`\n  - get-show: `test_e2e_show_*.py`, `test_yaml_output.py`\n  - set-writeback e2e (one per task 5.x): `test_set_targets.py` (5.2), `test_set_replacements.py` (5.7), `test_set_comments.py` (5.6), …\n  - set-writeback unit: `tests/writeback/test_<component>.py`, e.g. `test_planner_includes.py`, `test_target.py`, `test_layout.py`, `test_verify.py`\n  - file-sync and lineage: `test_e2e_reconcile.py`, `test_reconcile.py`, `test_e2e_lineage.py`\n\nSpecs are in `spec/spec/{config-foundation,config-get-show,config-set-writeback,config-file-sync,config-lineage}`.\n\n## 2. Includes today and the node model\n\n`loader.py`:\n- **Detection:** `_Builder.build` (l.417, check at l.424). Any string scalar value starting with `file:`, in any style, is an include. Keys never are.\n- **Loading:** `_Builder._include` (l.485). The target is `self._source.path.parent / spec`.\n  - A name not ending in `.yml`/`.yaml` is a TEXT include: a `ConfigScalar` holding the exact text, `style=None`, `origin=Origin(target, Span(0,len))`.\n  - Otherwise it is a YAML include. `_refuse_cycle` (l.511) checks the `chain`, `_compose` parses the target, and a new `_Builder(target, …, chain+target)` builds it. The result gets the include attached with `dataclasses.replace(embedded, include=ref)` (l.509). An empty target becomes a null scalar.\n- **Reading:** `_Loader.read_include` (l.313) raises `ConfigLoadError` for a missing or unreadable target, naming the including file and the key path. Each file is read once (diamonds are shared) and every file read is listed in `document.files`.\n- **Overrides:** the `overrides` dict on `load_config` (l.260) is keyed by real path and is what verification uses.\n\n`model.py` dataclasses (all frozen):\n- `SourceFile(path, real_path, text, line_ending)` (l.59)\n- `Span(start, end)`: character indices (l.69)\n- `Origin(file, span)` (l.77)\n- `IncludeKind` enum: `NONE`, `YAML`, `TEXT` (l.84)\n- `IncludeRef(kind, spec, target: SourceFile, directive: Origin)` (l.93). `directive` is the span of the `file:` scalar in the including file.\n- `ConfigScalar(value, style, origin, include=None)` (l.120)\n- `MappingEntry(key, key_origin, key_style, value)` (l.130)\n- `ConfigMapping(entries, style, origin, include=None)` (l.140), with `.get(key)`\n- `ConfigSequence(items, style, origin, include=None)` (l.157)\n- `ConfigDocument(root_file, root, files)` (l.171), with `include_kind()`\n\nAn include root's `origin` is in the target file; its children have `include=None` and origins in the target. **The model assumes every collection's entries share one file.** A merged node (entries from the target plus overrides from the including file) breaks that assumption, and `MappingEntry` has no per-entry provenance beyond `key_origin`.\n\n## 3. `$ref` today\n\nThere is no handling. `{$ref: file:x.yaml, model: big}` loads as a normal mapping, and the `file:x.yaml` value of `$ref` is expanded as an include in place. The result is `{ "$ref": <x's content>, model: big }`, and paths like `agents.coder.$ref.model` work.\n\nThings to decide in the loader:\n- where `$ref` detection goes (in `_build_mapping`, l.440)\n- duplicate-key and merge-key checks (l.456–469)\n- whether `$ref` may be a non-string, a text include or a non-mapping target\n- `$ref` at the document root: `load_config` requires the root to be a `ConfigMapping` (l.278); cycle detection also applies there\n- `$ref` inside an included file: the spec is relative to that file's directory\n- `$ref` inside a sequence item\n\n## 4. How `set` resolves its target\n\n**Pipeline:** `setter.set_value` (l.257):\n1. `resolve_target`\n2. a no-op check with `plain_equal(current, intended, ordered=False)`\n3. `plan_patch`\n4. `verify_patch`\n5. `diff_leaves`\n6. `write_patches` (atomic, rolls back on failure)\n7. `history.record_changes`\n\n**Target resolution:** `target.py`, `resolve_target` (l.104) calls `walk_path` and then `_target` (l.126):\n- `ReplaceTarget(node, parent, key)` (l.47): the path exists.\n- `AddKeyTarget(mapping, missing)` (l.57): the walk stopped at `MISSING_KEY` on a mapping, which is the deepest existing ancestor. Digit-only missing keys exit 5.\n- `AppendTarget(sequence)` (l.66): the index equals the list length.\n- Anything else is `PathNotFoundError` (exit 5) naming `walk.deepest.origin.file`.\n\nThe intended tree is built by `compare.splice` (l.119) over `node_to_plain`. `node_to_plain` (l.53) turns every include root into `IncludeDirective(text, content, kind)`. `_Splicer.at` (l.146) descends into a YAML directive's content, so a key added under an include stays inside the directive.\n\n**Planning:** `planner.py`, `plan_patch` (l.112) with `_Planner`:\n- **Existing value changed:** `_Planner.replace` (l.144). It uses `parent.origin.file` and `layout.entry_slot`/`item_slot`, then `change_slot` (l.301). That covers no-op, include, `_change_value`, `_replace_scalar` (l.727), `_rewrite_block` (l.619), `_merge` (l.403) and `_replace_value` (l.645). The file chosen is the file of the *parent* collection, so values inside an included file are patched there.\n- **New key:** `add_key` (l.160) wraps the intermediate keys into a nested `NewMapping`, then `add_entries(target.mapping, …)` (l.177). It uses `file = collection.origin.file`, so **today a new key under an included mapping goes into the included file** (test `test_new_key_below_yaml_include_is_added_in_the_included_file`, `tests/test_set_targets.py:87`). This is the decision point your change must alter.\n  - Block mappings use `_block_insertion` (l.208) with `layout.insertion_point` (`layout.py` l.346), `entries_column` (l.334), `indent_step()` and `dash_offset()`, and `render_block_entry`.\n  - `insertion_point` places new lines after the last entry's content, skipping trailing blank lines and comment lines indented at least to the entries column.\n  - The new key's quote style follows the last sibling (`_sibling_styles`, l.875).\n  - Flow mappings use `_flow_insertion` (l.242).\n- **List append:** `append` (l.171) calls `add_entries(sequence, ((None, value),))`.\n- **Whole include value replaced:** `_change_include` (l.360). A YAML include root raises `WriteRefusedError` (exit 3): "the whole value comes from the include file:…; change the included file instead", naming the target file and the path `at`. A text include gets the target's whole content replaced via `_replace_file` (l.815); a non-string VALUE is refused.\n- **Patch representation:**\n  - `TextEdit(start, end, text, kind: EditKind.INLINE|LINES)` (`edits.py` l.413), with character offsets into the original text.\n  - Edits are collected per real path in `_Planner._edits`; whole-file replacements go in `_whole`.\n  - `patches()` (l.828) calls `apply_edits` (`edits.py` l.422), which sorts edits, checks overlap and moves end-of-line comments of INLINE edits back to their original column. The result is one `FilePatch(file, new_text)` per changed file, in `document.files` order.\n  - `_add` (l.809) refuses a file that would be changed in two ways.\n- **Scalar replaced by a block mapping (exists):** `_replace_value` (l.645) is used for kind changes, including scalar to mapping.\n  - It finds the inline extent and the comment on that line.\n  - `render_block_value(value, owner_column, in_sequence, ctx, compact=comment is None)` (`collection_render.py` l.74) returns `(inline, following)`.\n  - If there is no inline part, the old inline text is removed. When there is no comment, the spaces after `:` are removed too. A comment is kept at its column via INLINE fix-up.\n  - The new following lines are inserted after the line (`_following_edit`, l.783).\n  - For an include root, `FileLayout.inline_extent` returns the **directive span** through `_directive` (`layout.py` l.396), and `body_lines` returns None. So this machinery could turn `coder: file:agents/coder.yaml  # c` into `coder:  # c\n    $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\n    top_p: 0.9`.\n  - The catch: `change_slot` sends include roots to `_change_include` before reaching `_change_value` (l.317), so a new path is needed.\n- **Read-back:** `verify.py`, `verify_patch` (l.352). It reloads the config with `load_config(config_file, overrides)` (all document files plus patched texts) and compares `plain_equal(node_to_plain(after.root), request.intended, ordered=True)`. A load error or a mismatch is a `WriteRefusedError` (exit 3). For your change, `node_to_plain` of a mapping-form include must equal what `splice` builds (e.g. the `IncludeDirective` content plus the override key in order). The intended-tree representation of mapping-form includes has to be designed in `compare.py`.\n\n**FileLayout constraints (`layout.py`):**\n- `entry_slot` and `item_slot` call `_require_here(entry.key_origin.file)` (l.235, l.254).\n- `insertion_point` requires `collection.origin.file` to be this file (l.355).\n- `_value_end` for mappings uses `node.entries[-1]` (l.475).\n- `_token_floors` (l.549), `_block_scalar_key_columns`, `_block_entries_in_file` (l.642) and `_detect_indent_step` (l.667) walk the tree assuming a node and its entries are in `node.origin.file`.\n\nA merged mapping with entries in two files would break `entry_slot`, `content_end`, `insertion_point` and `_directive` (l.398 raises ValueError "not written in"). Mapping-form overrides need their own origin/provenance or a separate model node.\n\n## 5. show, get, `--sources`, reconcile\n\n- **show:** `handle_show` resolves the node with `node_at` → `resolve_path` (`resolve.py` l.302), then `render_yaml`. Each scalar line is labelled with `node.origin.file` (`yaml_output._scalar_lines`, l.150–168); `key:` lines have no source. Labels come from `source_label`.\n  - Merged overrides would be labelled correctly as long as each override scalar keeps its own origin.\n  - A `$ref` key surviving in the tree would be printed as a key `$ref`; `_key_text` (l.225) decides the quoting.\n- **get:** `get_value` (`theatre.py` l.121). A `WrongNodeKindError` or `PathNotFoundError` names `node.origin.file` or `walk.deepest.origin.file`. For a merged mapping, the choice of origin file decides which file error messages name.\n- **Leaf flattening:** `flatten_leaves` (`resolve.py` l.318) is a plain recursion over entries and items. `reconcile_changes` (`reconcile.py` l.366) and `diff_leaves` (`leaf_diff.py` l.419) compare by dotted path and value only. They need no special handling as long as the merged tree drops the `$ref` key. If `$ref` stays as a node, leaves would appear as `x.$ref.model`.\n\n## 6. Errors and exit codes (`errors.py`)\n\n`ExitCode`: OK=0, USAGE=2, WRITE_REFUSED=3, LOAD=4, PATH=5. The code is a class attribute:\n\n| Class | Exit | Line |\n|---|---|---|\n| `UsageError` | 2 | l.668 |\n| `WriteRefusedError` | 3 | l.674 |\n| `ConfigLoadError` | 4 | l.680 |\n| `PathError` | 5 | l.686 |\n| `PathNotFoundError`, `WrongNodeKindError` | 5 | l.692, l.696 |\n\nMessage format is `<file>: <path>: <problem>` (`format_message`, l.642).\n\nExit 4 is raised in `loader.py`:\n- `read_source` (l.310)\n- `read_include` (l.323, l.331)\n- `_decode` (l.356)\n- `_compose` (l.384, l.387)\n- the top-level-not-a-mapping check (l.280)\n- `_build_mapping` (complex key, merge key, duplicate key)\n- `_refuse_cycle` (l.519)\n- `_refuse_properties` (anchor or tag)\n- `locate.find_config_file`\n\nInside `set`, `verify_patch` wraps every `ConfigLoadError` into exit 3. `theatre.open_theatre` (l.86) loads before the store is opened, so a load error creates no history state.\n\n## 7. Test conventions\n\n- **Fixtures:** `make_theatre(config, *, config_name, files={rel_path: str|bytes}, name)` writes `<tmp>/theatre/.troupe/troupe.yml` and returns the theatre directory. `run_cli(*args, cwd)` runs the real installed `troupe` script and returns `CliResult(exit_code, stdout: bytes, stderr: str)`.\n- **set e2e:** `run_set(theatre, path, value)`, `snapshot_tree(theatre)` (excludes `history.sqlite*`) and `assert_file_bytes(path, expected)` for exact UTF-8 byte comparison. `test_set_targets.py` defines `_assert_success`, `_assert_only_changed(theatre, before, rel, expected)` and `_assert_refused(..., code, file=, path=, names=)`, which checks a single stderr line starting with `f"{theatre/file}: {path}: "`.\n- **Existing fixtures for your scenario:** `test_set_targets.py:65` has `AGENTS_ROOT = "model: gpt\nagents:\n  coder: file:agents/coder.yaml\n"` and `CODER_FILE = ".troupe/agents/coder.yaml"`.\n- **Planner unit tests:** `tests/writeback/test_planner_includes.py` has `_patches(tmp_path, files, path, value)` (l.35), which returns `[(file name, new_text)]` and itself checks file order, no double patch and read-back equality. `_refused(...)` (l.63) returns the `WriteRefusedError`.\n- **Traceability:** every docstring starts with `Req X.Y: ...`. Unit test modules name the task (e.g. "task 3.6").\n- **Loader tests:** `tests/test_loader_includes.py` (l.63–365) and e2e `tests/test_e2e_includes.py`, which has `_assert_failure(result, code, file, path)`.\n\n## 8. Other affected areas\n\nTests that will change meaning:\n- `test_new_key_below_yaml_include_is_added_in_the_included_file` (`test_set_targets.py:87`) expects the key to be written in the included file. Under your rule it would go in the including file.\n- `test_changing_a_yaml_include_root_is_refused` (l.109) covers the exit-3 refusal; decide how mapping-form `{model: x}` over an include root behaves.\n- `test_planner_includes.py`: `test_dropped_yaml_include_removes_only_the_directive_entry` (l.150) and `test_unchanged_yaml_include_inside_a_merge_is_untouched` (l.138).\n\nOther areas to cover:\n- **`set` of an existing override key:** `ReplaceTarget.parent.origin.file` would be the include target. The parent of an override must resolve to the including file.\n- **Removing an override entry during a merge:** `_delete_entries` and `_block_deletions` (l.535) use `layout.entry_slot`, which requires the key to be in this file.\n- **`file:` VALUE writes:** `new_to_plain` (l.75) makes `IncludeDirective(text, None)`. A mapping VALUE `{$ref: file:x.yaml, a: 1}` has no special meaning today, and `splice.place` never merges into YAML directives.\n- **History:** `diff_leaves` and reconcile see only leaves, but the changed leaf set must exclude any `$ref` key.\n- **Diamonds and shared targets:** one target included at several places is one shared `SourceFile`. Overrides are per include site, so the shared subtree's nodes must not be mutated.\n- **Watch:** `Watcher._watch_paths` (`watch.py` l.112) uses `document.files`, which already includes `$ref` targets if the loader reads them through `_Loader`.\n- **`layout._token_floors`** (l.562–570) treats a node not in this file whose `include.directive` is here as a single token. Inside a mapping-form include, the `$ref:` key and the override keys in the including file must also be visited so comment detection keeps working.\n- **`$ref` at the document root:** `load_config` (l.277) and `resolve_target` (l.115) require the root to be a mapping. `FileLayout.insertion_point` for root-level overrides needs a root origin in the root file.
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; sed -n 150,200p spec/spec/config-set-writeback/requirements.md; grep -n \"Step [0-9]\\|step-[0-9] note\\|(step [0-9])\" spec/spec/config-foundation/requirements.md | head"}
tool: Write {"content": "# Brief: config-include-overrides\n\n## Problem\nIncluded YAML files are shared between projects (for example one `agents/coder.yaml` used by several\ntheatres). Today `troupe config set` writes a new key under an included section into the included file itself\n(step 1 rule 2: \"a new key is written into the file containing the deepest existing ancestor mapping\"). One\nproject's `set` therefore silently changes every other project that shares the file. Developers have no way\nto say \"this theatre's coder is the shared coder plus `temperature: 0.3`\" without copying the shared file.\n\n## Current State\n- config-foundation (`config/loader.py`): any string scalar starting with `file:` is an include. `.yml`/`.yaml`\n  targets are embedded (`IncludeKind.YAML`), anything else is a text include. The source-mapped model\n  (`config/model.py`: `SourceFile`, `Span`, `Origin`, `IncludeRef(kind, spec, target, directive)`,\n  `ConfigScalar`/`ConfigMapping`/`ConfigSequence` with `origin` and `include`) assumes every collection's entries\n  are written in one file. There is no `$ref` handling: `{$ref: file:x.yaml, model: big}` loads today as an\n  ordinary mapping with a key `$ref` whose value is the embedded file.\n- config-set-writeback (`config/writeback/`): `resolve_target` → `AddKeyTarget(mapping, missing)`; the planner's\n  `add_entries` writes into `collection.origin.file`, so a new key under an included mapping lands in the included\n  file (pinned by `tests/test_set_targets.py::test_new_key_below_yaml_include_is_added_in_the_included_file`).\n  Replacing a YAML include root is refused with exit 3 (`_change_include`). `_replace_value` can already turn a\n  scalar (including an include directive, via `FileLayout.inline_extent`) into a block mapping while keeping the\n  key line's end-of-line comment. Read-back verification (`verify_patch`) reloads the patched files and compares\n  `node_to_plain(root)` with the intended tree from `compare.splice`, where incl...[truncated 9713 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='spec/steering/roadmap.md'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"\"\"- [x] config-lineage -- (step 4)\"\"\",\"\"\"- [x] config-lineage -- (step 4)\"\"\")\ns=s.rstrip('\\n')+'''\n\n## Step 5 — Keep shared includes shared (CR-A, 2026-10-05)\n\n### Overview\nIncluded files are shared between projects, so adding a new setting under an included section must no longer\nmodify the shared file. The step 5 contract section \"Overrides\" replaces rule 2 under `set PATH VALUE`: an include\nmay be written in mapping form (`{$ref: \"file:…\", <overrides>}`, value = included content deep-merged with the\noverrides); a new key whose deepest existing ancestor comes from an included file is written as an override in the\nincluding file (a scalar-form include is first converted to mapping form, keeping the key line's comment); list\nappends and new keys inside list items still go to the file holding the list; a mapping-form include of a non-YAML\nfile with siblings → exit 4; an overridden existing value is changed in the override, a non-overridden one where it\ncomes from.\n\n### Approach Decision\n- **Chosen**: the loader builds the merged view (`$ref` never appears in it), and each merged mapping keeps its\n  physical layers (base mapping in the included file, override mapping and `$ref` directive in the including\n  file). The writeback layer resolves the physical target through the layers; new keys under an include become\n  overrides of the nearest include above the deepest existing ancestor unless a list item comes first. Read-back\n  verification models mapping-form directives in the intended tree.\n- **Why**: every reader (`get`, `show --sources`, leaves, reconcile, history, watch) already works on the loaded\n  tree, so a merged tree with per-scalar origins gives them the contract's value for free; only the writer needs\n  the physical layers, and they stay attached to the nodes they describe.\n- **Rejected alternatives**: merging ...[truncated 1571 chars]

Included\nYAML files are shared between projects. A theatre must be able to adjust an included section for itself without\nchanging the shared file, and `troupe config set` must do so on its own when it adds a new setting under an\nincluded section.\n\nTwo things change:\n- **Reading**: an include may be written in *mapping form*, a mapping with a `$ref` key whose value is the include\n  string. Its other keys are *overrides*. The value at that place is the included content deep-merged with the\n  overrides. Every command that reads the configuration sees this merged value.\n- **Writing**: a new key below an included section is written as an override in the *including* file. Existing\n  settings are still changed where they live: in the override when they are overridden, otherwise in the file they\n  come from.\n\nTerms used below:\n- **Include string**: a string starting with `file:`, as defined by config-foundation Requirement 4.\n- **Scalar-form include**: an include written as an include string at a value's place (step 1).\n- **Mapping-form include**: a mapping written in a YAML file that has a key `$ref` whose value is an include string.\n  Its other keys are its **overrides**. tool: Write {"content": "# Research & Design Decisions: config-include-overrides\n\n## Summary\n- **Feature**: `config-include-overrides` (step 5, CR-A \"keep shared includes shared\", contract section \"Overrides\")\n- **Discovery Scope**: Extension (light discovery: integration points in the loader, model and writeback)\n- **Key Findings**:\n  - Every reader (`get`, `show`, `--sources`, leaf flattening, reconcile, history, watch) works on the loaded\n    tree by path and per-node `origin`. If the loader produces the *merged* value with correct per-scalar\n    origins and no `$ref` key, readers need no change.\n  - The writer (`writeback/`) assumes that a collection and all its entries are written in one file\n    (`FileLayout.entry_slot`, `insertion_point`, `_value_end`, and the tree walks `_token_floors`,\n    `_block_entries_in_file`, `_block_scalar_key_columns`). A merged mapping breaks that assumption, so the\n    writer must reach the *written* (physical) mappings behind a merged one.\n  - The existing scalar → block-collection machinery (`_replace_value`) almost performs the scalar-form →\n    mapping-form conversion, but it re-renders the value (the include string's bytes must be kept) and moves the\n    key line's comment back to its column (config-set-writeback 11.5), while the contract example moves it next to\n    the `:`. The conversion therefore needs its own small edit plan.\n\n## Research Log\n\n### How includes and set targets work today\n- **Context**: where step 5 plugs in.\n- **Findings**:\n  - `loader._Builder.build` turns any string scalar starting with `file:` into an include root\n    (`dataclasses.replace(embedded, include=IncludeRef(...))`). There is no `$ref` handling. A `$ref` key is an\n    ordinary key today, and its `file:` value is expanded in place.\n  - `target.resolve_target` → `AddKeyTarget(mapping, missing)`. `planner.add_entries` writes into\n    `collection.origin.file`, which is the included file for a mapping inside an include. This is ...[truncated 10153 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "# Design Document: config-include-overrides\n\n## Overview\nStep 5 lets a theatre adjust a shared included YAML file without changing it. An include may be written in\nmapping form, `{$ref: \"file:…\", <overrides>}`. Its value is the included content deep-merged with the overrides.\n`troupe config set` writes a new key below an included section as an override in the including file. Where the\ninclude is still in scalar form, it is first converted to mapping form.\n\nThe loader builds the **merged view** as the configuration tree. Every reader (`get`, `show`, `--sources`,\nhistory, reconcile, `watch`) therefore sees the contract's value unchanged. Each merged mapping keeps its\n**layers**: the included mapping and the override mapping as written. The writer uses these to find the file and\nthe written mapping behind a merged value.\n\n### Goals\n- Load mapping-form includes and deep-merge their overrides (Req 1), with the exit-4 refusals (Req 2).\n- The merged configuration is what every command reads and records (Req 3).\n- New keys under includes go to the including file as overrides (Req 4, 5, 6). Existing values change where they\n  are written (Req 7).\n- `$ref` is reserved (Req 8). Every write keeps the step 1 safety guarantees (Req 9).\n\n### Non-Goals\n- Deleting an included key through an override, overriding list items, `$ref` to anything but `file:` strings.\n- Any change to rendering rules, events, lineage, reconcile or `show` formats.\n\n## Boundary Commitments\n\n### This Spec Owns\n- Recognising, validating and deep-merging mapping-form includes in the loader, and the model fields that record\n  their layers (`IncludeRef.form`, `ConfigMapping.layers`, `MergeLayers`).\n- The helpers that map a merged value to the mappings written in files (`config/overrides.py`).\n- Write-target placement for new keys (the replaced rule 2), and the physical target of changed and removed\n  values below merged mappings.\n- The scalar-form → mapping-form conv...[truncated 20666 chars]

Records the placement walk, pruning of emptied\noverrides and the conversion's comment handling in research.md.\"; git log --oneline -1"}

tool: Write {"content": "# Implementation Plan\n\n**Conventions.** Every test written for this spec names the requirement IDs it proves in its docstring (for\nexample `\"\"\"Req 4.1: ...\"\"\"`), as in the earlier specs. Tests use real theatres on disk and the real CLI\n(`testing.md`). Preservation tests compare whole files byte for byte. Each task ends with\n`uv run pytest -n auto`, `uv run mypy --strict .`, `uv run ruff check .` and `uv run ruff format --check .`\npassing.\n\n**External prerequisites.** config-foundation, config-set-writeback, config-file-sync, config-get-show and\nconfig-lineage are complete. No new runtime dependency is added.\n\n- [ ] 1. Foundation: the layered model and its lookups\n- [ ] 1.1 Record mapping-form includes and merged mappings in the model, and provide the deep merge and the written-mapping lookups\n  - The model records the `$ref` mapping as written for a mapping-form include, and both layers (included side, override side) for every merged mapping. Scalar-form includes and unmerged nodes look exactly as before. The reserved key `$ref` has one named home.\n  - The deep merge keeps the included keys in their order, replaces an overridden value in place (merging recursively where both sides are mappings), appends the override-only keys in their order, never carries the `$ref` entry, and never mutates its inputs.\n  - The lookups give, for a key of a merged mapping, the written mapping and entry that hold its value (override side first). They also give every written mapping that holds a key, with the override chain leading to it, and the written parts of a node for walks over the files.\n  - Done when unit tests over real loaded mappings prove the merge result and order (nested mappings, scalar over mapping, list replaces, mapping over scalar), that overridden scalars keep their own origin, and each lookup for override-only, included-only and two-sided keys\n  - _Requirements: 1.2, 1.3, 1.5, 7.1, 7.2_\n\n- [ ] 2. Core: loading mapping-form incl...[truncated 9220 chars]

Each merged mapping remembers its included part and its override part, so `set` can find which file to write. The reasons are recorded in `research.md`.\n- **Tasks:** 16 tasks. If it exists only in an override, the step-1 rule points to the including file.\n- **No include involved:** the flag is accepted and changes nothing, rather than being refused.\n- **Parsing:** the flag takes no value, can go anywhere among the arguments, and works together with `--reason`. `--in-include=TEXT` is a usage error (exit 2).\n\nTo finish both change requests, run:\n\n```\n/method-command config-include-overrides\n```

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Mapping-form include overrides: the deep merge and the written-mapping lookups\n(config-include-overrides Requirements 1.2, 1.3, 1.5, 7.1, 7.2).\n\nA mapping-form include ``{$ref: \"file:...\", <overrides>}`` with overrides has as its value the deep\nmerge of the included content with the overrides. The loader builds that merged mapping with\n:func:`deep_merge`; every merged mapping keeps both sides in ``ConfigMapping.layers``. The writer\nuses the lookups here to reach the mappings actually written in files behind a merged one:\n\n* :func:`physical_entry`: the written mapping and entry that hold a key's merged value;\n* :func:`physical_holders`: every written mapping holding a key, with the layers leading to it;\n* :func:`holder_of`: the entry holding a given written mapping in a merged parent (for pruning\n  emptied nested overrides, research.md Decision 4);\n* :func:`written_parts`: what is written for a node, for walks over the files.\n\nThis module is pure: it performs no I/O and never mutates or rebuilds its inputs.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport dataclasses\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom enum import Enum\n\nfrom troupe.config.constants import INCLUDE_REF_KEY\nfrom troupe.config.model import ConfigMapping, ConfigNode, IncludeRef, MappingEntry\n\n\ndef is_ref_key(key: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return whether ``key`` is the reserved key of a mapping-form include.\"\"\"\n    return key == INCLUDE_REF_KEY\n\n\ndef override_entries(mapping: ConfigMapping) -> tuple[MappingEntry, ...]:\n    \"\"\"Return the entries of ``mapping`` without its ``$ref`` entry, in written order.\"\"\"\n    return tuple(entry for entry in mapping.entries if not is_ref_key(entry.key))\n\n\ndef _override(mapping: ConfigMapping, key: str) -> MappingEntry | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the override entry of ``key`` in ``mapping``; the ``$ref`` entry is never one.\"\"\"\n    return None if is_ref_key(key) else mapping.get(key)\n\n\ndef deep_merge(\n    base: ConfigMapping, over: Conf...[truncated 6373 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Loading mapping-form includes ``{$ref: \"file:...\", <overrides>}`` (config-include-overrides\nRequirement 1, task 2.1).\n\nEvery test writes a real theatre with include files and loads it with ``load_config``. A bare\nmapping-form include must load to the same plain value as the scalar form; one with overrides loads\nto the deep merge of the included content with the overrides. No ``$ref`` key is ever part of the\nloaded value.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport hashlib\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import MakeTheatre\nfrom troupe.config.constants import INCLUDE_REF_KEY\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ConfigLoadError, ExitCode\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.model import (\n    ConfigDocument,\n    ConfigMapping,\n    ConfigNode,\n    ConfigScalar,\n    ConfigSequence,\n    IncludeKind,\n)\nfrom troupe.config.resolve import flatten_leaves\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import PlainValue, node_to_plain\n\nCODER = \"\"\"\\\nmodel: small  # shared\nparams:\n  temperature: 0.7\n  top_p: 0.9\ntools: [read, write]\nname: coder\nretries: 2\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef _config(theatre: Path) -> Path:\n    return theatre / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\"\n\n\ndef _load(make_theatre: MakeTheatre, config: str, files: dict[str, str], name: str) -> Path:\n    return _config(make_theatre(config, files=files, name=name))\n\n\ndef _node_at(document: ConfigDocument, *keys: str | int) -> ConfigNode:\n    node: ConfigNode = document.root\n    for key in keys:\n        if isinstance(key, int):\n            assert isinstance(node, ConfigSequence)\n            node = node.items[key]\n        else:\n            assert isinstance(node, ConfigMapping)\n            entry = node.get(key)\n            assert entry is not None, key\n            node = entry.value\n    return node\n\n\ndef _mapping_at(document: ConfigDocument, *keys: str | int...[truncated 18605 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\nimport re\nfor p in ['tests/test_overrides_merge.py','tests/writeback/test_overrides_lookup.py']:\n    s=open(p).read()\n    old='    over = _mapping(_value(document.root, \"over\"))\\n'\n    assert old in s, p\n    s=s.replace(old,'''    # The loader merges ``over`` itself (task 2.1); its ``$ref`` mapping as written is the form.\n    loaded_over = _value(document.root, \"over\")\n    assert loaded_over.include is not None\n    over = _mapping(loaded_over.include.form)\n''')\n    s=s.replace('''The loader does not recognise ``$ref`` yet (task 2.1), so each case loads real files from a\ntemporary theatre with the existing loader and merges''','''Each case loads real files from a temporary theatre and merges''') if 'merge.py' in p else s.replace('''The loader does not recognise ``$ref`` yet (task 2.1), so each case loads real files from a\ntemporary theatre with the existing loader and builds the merged mappings with ``deep_merge``, as\nthe loader will do for ``{$ref: ..., <overrides>}``.''','''Each case loads real files from a temporary theatre and builds the merged mappings with\n``deep_merge`` from the ``$ref`` mapping as written (``include.form``), as the loader does for\n``{$ref: ..., <overrides>}``.''')\n    open(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nsed -n 1,12p tests/test_overrides_merge.py"}

tool: Edit {"file_path": "/work/repo/src/troupe/config/loader.py", "new_string": "included several times (a diamond) is shared and listed once in ``ConfigDocument.files``.\n\nMapping-form includes (config-include-overrides Requirement 1): a mapping with a key whose text is\n:data:`INCLUDE_REF_KEY` (any key style) is an include of that key's value, which is kept as written\n(never expanded) and must be an include string. The mapping as written is ``IncludeRef.form``; its\nother entries, the overrides, are built as usual (so they may hold includes themselves). A bare\n``$ref`` mapping loads exactly as the scalar form of the same string, YAML or text. With overrides,\nthe value is :func:`troupe.config.overrides.deep_merge` of the included mapping with the form, so\nno ``$ref`` key is part of the loaded value. Overrides on a non-YAML target, on a YAML target\nwhose content is not a mapping, and a ``$ref`` value that is not an include string are refused.\nThis works wherever a value may be written, including the document root (which must still load\nas a mapping).\n", "old_string": "included several times (a diamond) is shared and listed once in ``ConfigDocument.files``.\n", "replace_all": false}

$ref: file:notes.md\\\\n    k: 1\\\\n\", \"items.0\"),\n        (\"$ref: file:notes.md\\\\nk: 1\\\\n\", None),\n    ],\n)\ndef test_text_target_with_overrides_names_file_path_and_target(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre, config: str, path: str | None\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 2.1: overrides on a non-YAML target name the including file, path and target.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(config, files={\".troupe/notes.md\": \"notes\\\\n\"})\n    error = _refusal(_config(theatre))\n    assert error.file == _config(theatre)\n    assert error.path == path\n    assert str(theatre / \".troupe\" / \"notes.md\") in error.problem\n    assert \"overrides apply only to YAML includes\" in error.problem\n\n\ndef test_text_target_refusal_in_an_included_file_names_that_file(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 2.1: a refused `$ref` mapping inside an included file names the included file.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(\n        \"agents: file:sub/agents.yml\\\\n\",\n        files={\n            \".troupe/sub/agents.yml\": \"coder:\\\\n  $ref: file:prompt.txt\\\\n  k: 1\\\\n\",\n            \".troupe/sub/prompt.txt\": \"p\\\\n\",\n        },\n    )\n    error = _refusal(_config(theatre))\n    assert ...[truncated 3627 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/test_loader_overrides.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold='''    \"\"\"Req 2.3: only overrides need a mapping; a bare `$ref` to a list loads like the scalar form.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(\n        \"a: {$ref: file:list.yml}\\\\nb: {$ref: file:empty.yml}\\\\n\",\n        files={\".troupe/list.yml\": \"- x\\\\n\", \".troupe/empty.yml\": \"\"},\n    )\n    assert _plain(load_config(_config(theatre))) == {\"a\": [\"x\"], \"b\": None}\n'''\nnew='''    \"\"\"Req 2.3: only overrides need a mapping; a bare `$ref` to a list or empty file loads.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(\n        \"a: {$ref: file:list.yml}\\\\nb: {$ref: file:empty.yml}\\\\n\",\n        files={\".troupe/list.yml\": \"- x\\\\n\", \".troupe/empty.yml\": \"\"},\n    )\n    document = load_config(_config(theatre))\n    assert _scalar_at(document, \"a\", 0).value == \"x\"\n    assert _scalar_at(document, \"b\").value is None\n'''\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/test_loader_overrides.py 2>&1 | tail -8"}
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Mapping-form includes that cannot mean anything, end to end through the real ``troupe`` script.\n\n``troupe config get`` on a theatre holding a refused ``$ref`` mapping exits 4 with an empty\nstdout and one stderr line ``<file>: <path>: <problem>`` (2.1, 2.2, 2.3); like any configuration\nthat cannot be loaded, no file is changed and no history store is created (2.4).\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom set_support import snapshot_tree\nfrom support import CliResult, MakeTheatre, RunCli\n\nTEXT_TARGET = \"agents:\\n  coder:\\n    $ref: file:prompt.md\\n    temperature: 0.3\\n\"\nNOT_AN_INCLUDE = \"agents:\\n  coder:\\n    $ref: coder.yaml\\n    temperature: 0.3\\n\"\nLIST_TARGET = \"agents:\\n  coder:\\n    $ref: file:coder.yaml\\n    temperature: 0.3\\n\"\n\n\ndef _get(run_cli: RunCli, theatre: Path, path: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", \"get\", path, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _refused(run_cli: RunCli, theatre: Path, file: Path, path: str, get_path: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Run ``get get_path`` and assert a load refusal naming ``file`` and ``path``.\n\n    Asserts exit 4, empty stdout, one stderr line, no file changed and no history state. Returns\n    the problem text.\n    \"\"\"\n    before = snapshot_tree(theatre)\n    result = _get(run_cli, theatre, get_path)\n    prefix = f\"{file}: {path}: \"\n    assert result.exit_code == 4, result.stderr\n    assert result.stdout == b\"\"\n    assert result.stderr.startswith(prefix), result.stderr\n    assert result.stderr.endswith(\"\\n\")\n    assert result.stderr.count(\"\\n\") == 1, result.stderr\n    assert snapshot_tree(theatre) == before\n    assert not list((theatre / \".troupe\").glob(\"history.sqlite*\"))\n    return result.stderr[len(prefix) : -1]\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"get_path\", [\"agents.coder.temperature\", \"other\"])\ndef test_o...[truncated 3817 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/config/loader.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold='''            if not isinstance(embedded, ConfigMapping):\n                raise ConfigLoadError(\n                    f\"overrides need an included mapping; {target_path} does not hold a mapping\",\n'''\nnew='''            if not isinstance(embedded, ConfigMapping):\n                if embedded is None:\n                    kind = \"nothing (the file is empty)\"\n                else:\n                    kind = \"a list\" if isinstance(embedded, ConfigSequence) else \"a scalar\"\n                raise ConfigLoadError(\n                    f\"overrides need a mapping; the included file {target_path} holds {kind}\",\n'''\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/test_e2e_overrides_refusals.py tests/test_loader_overrides.py 2>&1 | tail -3"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Every command reads the merged configuration, end to end through the real ``troupe`` script.\n\nA mapping-form include (``{$ref: \"file:…\", <overrides>}``) loads as the included mapping\ndeep-merged with its overrides, and that merged view is the configuration every reader sees:\n``get`` and ``show`` print it (3.1); ``show --sources`` labels an overridden scalar with the\nincluding file and every other included scalar with the included file (3.2); import and reconcile\nrecord its leaves, so a hand edit of an override is recorded as the ``set``/``remove`` events of\nthe merged leaves it changes (3.3) and rewriting an include between scalar and bare mapping form\nrecords nothing (3.4); ``watch`` follows ``$ref`` targets (3.5). ``$ref`` is not part of the merged\nvalue, so a ``get``/``show`` PATH with a ``$ref`` segment is not found (8.3).\n\n``history`` never reconciles, so it is the safe observer of what import and reconcile recorded.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport signal\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom set_support import snapshot_tree\nfrom support import CliResult, MakeTheatre, RunCli, step1_members, write_theatre\nfrom watch_support import HistoryEvent, WatchProcess\n\nROOT = \"\"\"\\\nname: demo\nagents:\n  coder:\n    $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\n    temperature: 0.3\n    params:\n      top_p: 0.9\n    extra: on\ncrew:\n  - $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\n    model: haiku\n\"\"\"\n\"\"\"A mapping-form include with an overridden scalar, a nested merged mapping and an override-only\nkey, and a mapping-form include as a list item.\"\"\"\n\nCODER = \"model: opus\\ntemperature: 0.7\\nparams:\\n  top_k: 5\\n  top_p: 0.5\\ntools: [grep]\\n\"\n\nFILES = {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": CODER}\n\nCODER_MERGED = (\n    b\"model: opus\\n\"\n    b\"temperature: 0.3\\n\"\n    b\"params:\\n\"\n    b\"  top_k: 5\...[truncated 16870 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; run(){ uv run pytest tests/test_e2e_overrides_read.py -q -n auto 2>&1 | grep -E \"^FAILED|[0-9]+ (passed|failed)\" | sed 's/tests\\/test_e2e_overrides_read.py:://'; }\necho \"== RED 2: origin of overriding scalar takes the included file\"\npython3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/config/overrides.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"\"\"        else:\n            entries.append(overriding)\n\"\"\",\"\"\"        else:\n            entries.append(dataclasses.replace(overriding, value=dataclasses.replace(overriding.value, origin=entry.value.origin)))  # SABOTAGE\n\"\"\",1)\ns=s.replace(\"\"\"    entries.extend(entry for entry in override_entries(over) if base.get(entry.key) is None)\"\"\",\"\"\"    entries.extend(dataclasses.replace(e, value=dataclasses.replace(e.value, origin=base.origin)) if not isinstance(e.value, ConfigMapping) else e for e in override_entries(over) if base.get(e.key) is None)  # SABOTAGE\"\"\")\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nrun; cp /tmp/ov.bak src/troupe/config/overrides.py\necho \"== RED 3: merged order puts override-only keys first\"\npython3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/config/overrides.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"\"\"    entries.extend(entry for entry in override_entries(over) if base.get(entry.key) is None)\"\"\",\"\"\"    entries[:0] = [entry for entry in override_entries(over) if base.get(entry.key) is None]  # SABOTAGE\"\"\")\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nrun; cp /tmp/ov.bak src/troupe/config/overrides.py\necho \"== RED 4: \\$ref not recognised by the loader\"\ncp src/troupe/config/loader.py /tmp/ld.bak\nsed -i 's/            if is_ref_key(key):/            if False and is_ref_key(key):  # SABOTAGE/' src/troupe/config/loader.py\nrun; cp /tmp/ld.bak src/troupe/config/loader.py\ngit status --short", "description": "RED 2-4: break origins, order, $ref detection", "timeout": 600000}

tool: Bash {"command": "mkdir -p /tmp/p31 && cd /tmp/p31 && rm -rf * && cat > c.yaml <<'EOF'\nmodel: m\ntemperature: 0.7\nparams:\n  a: 1\nEOF\ncat > troupe.yml <<'EOF'\nagents:\n  coder:\n      $ref: \"file:c.yaml\"   # the coder\n      temperature: 0.3  # hot\n      params:\n          b: '# not'\n  lead: x  # keep\nother: 1\nEOF\ncd /work/repo && uv run python - <<'EOF'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.layout import FileLayout\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_patch\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nd = load_config(Path(\"/tmp/p31/troupe.yml\"))\nl = FileLayout.build(d.root_file, d)\nprint(l.indent_step(), [l.comment_start(i) for i in range(l.line_count)])\nfor path in [\"agents.lead\", \"other\"]:\n    p = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    try:\n        r = resolve_target(d, p, parse_value(\"y\", p))\n        for patch in plan_patch(d, r): print(repr(patch.new_text))\n    except Exception as e: print(\"ERR\", type(e), e)\nEOF"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Unit tests of the FileLayout in files with mapping-form includes (task 3.1):\n``troupe.config.writeback.layout`` over ``$ref`` mappings and merged mappings.\n\nEach case loads a real theatre configuration with the real loader and analyses one of its files.\nAt the slot of a mapping-form include root the layout uses the ``$ref`` mapping as written; the\nwalks for comments, indentation step, dash offset and block-scalar owner columns visit the written\nparts of merged mappings and ``$ref`` mappings. A planner-level case proves that an existing-value\nchange next to a ``$ref`` mapping leaves its lines untouched.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Mapping\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.model import (\n    ConfigDocument,\n    ConfigMapping,\n    ConfigNode,\n    ConfigSequence,\n    SourceFile,\n    Span,\n)\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import node_to_plain, plain_equal\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.layout import FileLayout, InsertionPoint, ValueSlot\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_patch\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\n\nROOT = \"troupe.yml\"\nCODER = \"model: m\\ntemperature: 0.7\\nparams:\\n    a: 1\\n\"\n\n\ndef _load(\n    tmp_path: Path, config: str, files: Mapping[str, str] | None = None\n) -> ConfigDocument:\n    for name, text in {**(files or {\"c.yaml\": CODER}), ROOT: config}.items():\n        target = tmp_path / name\n        target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        target.write_bytes(text.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n    return load_config(tmp_path / ROOT)\n\n\ndef _file(document: ConfigDocument, name: str) -> SourceFile:\n    for source in document.files:\n        if source.path.name == name:\n            return source\n...[truncated 14561 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/test_loader_overrides.py'\ns=open(p).read()\na=\"def test_scalar_overrides_a_mapping_and_mapping_overrides_a_scalar(\"\nnew='''def test_included_side_keeps_the_include_of_a_target_whose_root_is_a_ref_mapping(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 1.6, 1.2: overrides on a file whose root is a bare `$ref` mapping keep that inner include.\n\n    The included side of the merge is the middle file's own include, so its `$ref` mapping stays\n    reachable for writers (the middle file's lines are written text like any other).\n    \"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(\n        \"coder:\\\\n  $ref: file:mid.yaml\\\\n  temperature: 0.3\\\\n\",\n        files={\".troupe/mid.yaml\": \"$ref: file:base.yaml\\\\n\", \".troupe/base.yaml\": \"model: gpt\\\\n\"},\n    )\n    document = load_config(_config(theatre))\n    coder = _mapping_at(document, \"coder\")\n    mid = (theatre / \".troupe\" / \"mid.yaml\").resolve()\n\n    assert coder.layers is not None\n    assert coder.include is not None and coder.include.spec == \"mid.yaml\"\n    inner = coder.layers.base.include\n    assert inner is not None\n    assert inner.spec == \"base.yaml\"\n    assert inner.form is not None and inner.form.origin.file.real_path == mid\n    assert _plain(document) == {\n        \"coder\": IncludeDirective(\"file:mid.yaml\", {\"model\": \"gpt\", \"temperature\": 0.3})\n    }\n\n\n'''\nassert a in s\ns=s.replace(a,new+a,1)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/test_loader_overrides.py -k inner_ 2>&1 | tail -3; uv run pytest -q tests/test_loader_overrides.py -k \"keeps_the_include\" 2>&1 | grep -E \"assert|passed|failed\" | head -5"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; mkdir -p /tmp/probe32 && cat > /tmp/probe32/p.py <<'EOF'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport tempfile\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import plain_equal, node_to_plain, new_to_plain\n\ndef run(root_text, files, path, value):\n    d = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())\n    (d/\"troupe.yaml\").write_text(root_text)\n    for k,v in files.items(): (d/k).write_text(v)\n    doc = load_config(d/\"troupe.yaml\")\n    p = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    req = resolve_target(doc, p, parse_value(value, p))\n    print(repr(value), \"current:\", req.current.get(path), \"intended:\", req.intended.get(path), \"noop:\", plain_equal(req.current, req.intended, ordered=False))\n\n# drop override\nrun(\"x:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n  k: 1\\n\", {\"a.yaml\": \"k: 0\\nj: 2\\n\"}, \"x\", '{$ref: \"file:a.yaml\"}')\nrun(\"x:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n  k: 1\\n\", {\"a.yaml\": \"k: 0\\nj: 2\\n\"}, \"x\", '{$ref: \"file:a.yaml\", k: 0}')\nrun(\"x:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n  k: 1\\n\", {\"a.yaml\": \"k: 0\\nj: 2\\n\"}, \"x\", '\"file:a.yaml\"')\n# scalar include current -> ref with override already matched\nrun(\"x: file:a.yaml\\n\", {\"a.yaml\": \"k: 0\\nj: 2\\n\"}, \"x\", '{$ref: \"file:a.yaml\", k: 0}')\n# nested override with scalar include value merged into included mapping\nrun(\"x:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n  m: file:m.yaml\\n\", {\"a.yaml\": \"m:\\n  p: 1\\n\", \"m.yaml\": \"q: 2\\n\"}, \"x\", '{$ref: \"file:a.yaml\", m: \"file:m.yaml\"}')\nd = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())\n(d/\"troupe.yaml\").write_text(\"x:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n  m: file:m.yaml\\n\"); (d/\"a.yaml\").write_text(\"m:\\n  p: 1\\n\"); (d/\"m.yaml\").write_text(\"q: 2\\n\")\nprint(node_to_plain(load_config(d/\"troupe.yaml\").root))\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/probe32/p.py"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/probe32/q.py <<'EOF'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport tempfile\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_patch\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.verify import verify_patch\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import root_plain\n\ndef run(root_text, files, path, value):\n    d = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())\n    (d/\"troupe.yaml\").write_text(root_text)\n    for k,v in files.items(): (d/k).write_text(v)\n    doc = load_config(d/\"troupe.yaml\")\n    p = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    req = resolve_target(doc, p, parse_value(value, p))\n    patches = plan_patch(doc, req)\n    print([pt.new_text for pt in patches])\n    try:\n        after = verify_patch(d/\"troupe.yaml\", doc, patches, req); print(\"OK\", root_plain(after))\n    except Exception as e: print(\"REFUSED\", type(e).__name__, e)\n\nrun(\"a: 1\\n\", {\"a.yaml\": \"m:\\n  p: 1\\n\", \"m.yaml\": \"q: 2\\n\"}, \"x\", '{$ref: \"file:a.yaml\", m: \"file:m.yaml\"}')\nrun(\"a: 1\\n\", {\"a.yaml\": \"j: 1\\n\", \"m.yaml\": \"q: 2\\n\"}, \"x\", '{$ref: \"file:a.yaml\", m: \"file:m.yaml\"}')\nrun(\"a: 1\\n\", {\"a.yaml\": \"m:\\n  p: 1\\n\", \"m.yaml\": \"q: 2\\n\"}, \"x\", '{$ref: \"file:a.yaml\", m: {$ref: \"file:m.yaml\", r: 3}}')\nrun(\"a: 1\\n\", {\"a.yaml\": \"j: 1\\n\", \"m.yaml\": \"q: 2\\n\"}, \"x\", '{$ref: \"file:a.yaml\", m: {$ref: \"file:m.yaml\", r: 3}}')\nrun(\"a: 1\\n\", {\"a.yaml\": \"l: [1]\\n\"}, \"x\", '{$ref: \"file:a.yaml\", l: [2, 3]}')\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/probe32/q.py"}

Fix this finding, keeping the original task context (task 3.2, Requirements 8.2, 9.2, boundary compare.py/verify.py + their tests; do not edit tasks.md, do not commit):\n\nFinding 1 (Req 8.2, 1.7): `_contains` wrongly refuses a valid write when an override is itself an include and the included file has a mapping under the same key — the loader merges the override include's content into the included mapping, so the loaded value is a plain dict without directive, and `plain_equal(IncludeDirective(\"file:m.yaml\", None), {...})` is False. Repro: root `a: 1`, a.yaml = `m: {p: 1}`, m.yaml = `q: 2`, `set x '{$ref: \"file:a.yaml\", m: \"file:m.yaml\"}'` → planner writes `x:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n  m: file:m.yaml\\n`, reads back `{m: {p: 1, q: 2}}`, still refused. Same with nested `m: {$ref: \"file:m.yaml\", r: 3}`.\nRemediation: in `_contains`, when the override value is an `IncludeDirective` without content and the loaded content[key] is a non-directive mapping (the merge absorbed it): if it has `overrides`, recurse `_contains(content[key], override.overrides, ...)`; if it is a plain scalar include, accept a mapping. Add test_compare cases (loaded content holds a merged dict under an include override, scalar and `$ref` forms) and a test_verify case for `{$ref: file:a.yaml, m: file:m.yaml}` where a.yaml has mapping `m`, asserting it verifies (and the nested `$ref` variant). Capture RED output for the new tests before fixing, rerun the full suite after your final edit (`uv run pytest -n auto -q`, Bash timeout 600000), mypy, ruff check, ruff format --check. Reply with the full `## Status Report` block including RED_PHASE_OUTPUT.", "recipient": "a90ae3e8a0254222f", "summary": "Fix 3.2 nested include containment", "to": "a90ae3e8a0254222f", "type": "message"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'PYEOF'\np='src/troupe/config/writeback/compare.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold='''    for key, override in overrides.items():\n        if key not in content:\n            return False\n        if isinstance(override, dict):\n            if not _contains(content[key], override, ordered=ordered):\n                return False\n        elif not plain_equal(override, content[key], ordered=ordered):\n            return False\n    return True\n'''\nnew='''    for key, override in overrides.items():\n        if key not in content:\n            return False\n        if not _contains_value(content[key], override, ordered=ordered):\n            return False\n    return True\n\n\ndef _contains_value(loaded: PlainValue, override: PlainValue, *, ordered: bool) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return whether the loaded value at an override's key holds the ``override``.\n\n    An include override (a contentless directive) whose key holds a mapping in the included file\n    is merged into that mapping by the loader (1.7), so the loaded value is a plain mapping: a\n    scalar-form include then holds (its content is not known here) and a ``$ref`` form needs its\n    own overrides contained in that mapping.\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(override, dict):\n        return _contains(loaded, override, ordered=ordered)\n    if isinstance(override, IncludeDirective) and override.content is None:\n        if isinstance(loaded, dict):\n            if override.overrides is None:\n                return True\n            return _contains(loaded, override.overrides, ordered=ordered)\n    return plain_equal(override, loaded, ordered=ordered)\n'''\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old,new)\ns=s.replace('''    ``content`` must be a mapping (or an include directive whose content is one) holding every\n    override key. tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; uv run ruff format --check . | tail -1; uv run ruff check . | tail -1; uv run mypy --strict . | tail -1; uv run pytest -n auto -q 2>&1 | tail -1; git status --short", "timeout": 600000}
assistant: I fixed the round-1 finding: a VALUE `$ref` with an include override now verifies when the loader merges that include into a mapping in the included file. After my last edit the full suite passed (2959), and mypy strict, ruff check and ruff format check are clean.\n\n**What was wrong.** I ran the repro and it behaved as reported. With `a.yaml` holding `m: {p: 1}`, the loader merges the override `m: file:m.yaml` into that mapping, so the read-back is a plain mapping `{p: 1, q: 2}` with no include marker. The old containment check compared the override include to that mapping, which can never be equal, so a correct write was refused.\n\n**The fix.** In `_contains`, each override value now goes through a new helper, `_contains_value`. The case for a contentless include override over a plain loaded mapping works like this:\n- A plain `file:` include is accepted, because the included file's own content can't be checked at this level.\n- A `$ref` form must have its own overrides contained in the merged mapping.\n- Mapping overrides still recurse, and every other value still needs `plain_equal`.\n\nA plain `file:` override passes whatever the included file holds. A `$ref` override only checks its own keys. If a stricter check of the included content is wanted, the comparison would need that content.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 3.2\n- TASK_BRIEF: `IncludeDirective.overrides`; `new_to_plain` turns a VALUE `$ref` whose value is a `file:` string into a contentless directive with overrides, and any other `$ref` stays a plain dict; `plain_equal` uses containment between a directive with overrides and one with content, and (round 1) an include override absorbed into a mapping by the merge is accepted (plain `file:` form) or must contain its own overrides (`$ref` form); `root_plain` unwraps a root include; `verify_patch` compares `root_plain(after)` with the intended configuration, accepting correct patches and refusing wrong ones with exit 3.\n- FILES_CHANGED: /work/repo/src/troupe/config/writeback/compare.py, /work/repo/src/troupe/config/writeback/verify.py, /work/repo/tests/writeback/test_compare.py, /work/repo/tests/writeback/test_verify.py\n- REQUIREMENTS_CHECKED: 8.2, 9.2 (plus 1.7 for the merge behaviour in the round-1 finding)\n- DESIGN_CHECKED: File layer > Compare (IncludeDirective.overrides, new_to_plain `$ref`, plain_equal containment, root_plain); File layer > Verify (root_plain); research.md Decision 5\n- RED_PHASE_OUTPUT: Round 1: `uv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_verify.py tests/writeback/test_compare.py` -> 3 failed, 86 passed: `FAILED test_verify.py::test_value_ref_with_an_include_override_merged_into_a_mapping_verifies[{$ref: "file:a.yaml", m: "file:m.yaml"}-expected0]` and `[... m: {$ref: "file:m.yaml", r: 3}}-expected1]`, both with `WriteRefusedError: .../troupe.yml: x: written configuration differs from the intended configuration`; `FAILED test_compare.py::test_include_override_absorbed_by_the_merge_is_contained` (`AssertionError: assert False`). Original round: ImportError on `root_plain` at collection, then `test_patched_root_ref_file_reading_back_as_intended_verifies` failed with WriteRefusedError ("differs from the intended configuration") before verify.py switched to `root_plain`.\n- TESTS_RUN: `uv run pytest -q tests/writeback` -> 1031 passed; `uv run pytest -n auto -q` (after the final edit) -> 2959 passed in 246.65s; `uv run mypy --strict .` -> no issues in 131 files; `uv run ruff check .` -> all checks passed; `uv run ruff format --check .` -> 132 files already formatted\n- CONCERNS: (1) Containment ignores key order even with `ordered=True`; non-mapping override values still respect `ordered`. (2) A plain `file:` include override absorbed into a mapping is accepted without checking the include's content, which is not available here; a `$ref` override absorbed this way checks only its own overrides. (3) Root-`$ref` verify tests build `SetRequest`/`ReplaceTarget` by hand because target resolution adopts `root_plain` in task 3.3 (`target.py` untouched).\n- EVIDENCE: compare.py: `_contains` -> `_contains_value` (contentless include override vs plain loaded mapping), `root_plain`, `new_to_plain`/`_include_text`, `plain_equal` overrides branches; verify.py uses `root_plain(after)`. New round-1 tests: test_compare.py::test_include_override_absorbed_by_the_merge_is_contained (plain `file:` and `$ref` forms both ways round, a differing override, a missing override key, an include override over a scalar), test_verify.py::test_value_ref_with_an_include_override_merged_into_a_mapping_verifies (the reviewer's repro and its nested `$ref` variant; read-back `x` equals `IncludeDirective("file:a.yaml", {"m": {"p": 1, "q": 2[, "r": 3]}})`).

Finding 1 from round 1 is resolved. `_contains_value` (`src/troupe/config/writeback/compare.py`) handles an include override with no content whose loaded value is a plain mapping because the merge absorbed it (Req 1.7):\n     - A plain `file:` include is accepted.\n     - A `$ref`-form include must contain its own overrides.\n     - Everything else still goes through `plain_equal`.\n     \n     I reran my reproduction script (`/tmp/probe32/q.py`). Both cases that were wrongly refused before (`m: "file:m.yaml"` and `m: {$ref: "file:m.yaml", r: 3}` over an included `m` mapping) now verify, and the read-back shows the expected merged mappings. The cases that already worked still work.\n  2. The new tests are adequate. `test_include_override_absorbed_by_the_merge_is_contained` covers both include forms, in both argument orders, with a differing override, a missing override, and an include override placed over a scalar (which is refused). `test_value_ref_with_an_include_override_merged_into_a_mapping_verifies` covers the end-to-end verify path for both forms.\n  3. FYI (accepted, unchanged from round 1): containment ignores the order of override keys, so verification does not pin where override-only keys land. Without the included content this cannot be checked strictly. The renderer writes keys in VALUE order, which is the actual safeguard.\n  4. FYI: the loose no-op check for directives without content (round 1, finding 3) is with the controller as an existing design-level item and is outside this task.\n  5. Directives without overrides behave as before: when `overrides is None` the original code paths run.\n- REMEDIATION: None.\n- SUMMARY: The round-1 fix makes include overrides that the merge absorbs into an included mapping verify correctly, with tests for both include forms, and the task meets Req 8.2/9.2 within its boundary with clean static checks.

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Unit tests of the PatchPlanner writing new keys at their placement (config-include-overrides\ntask 3.4): ``troupe.config.writeback.planner.plan_patch`` for add-key targets whose placement is a\nwritten mapping.\n\nA new key below an include is written as an override in the including file: into an existing\noverride (the override side of a merged mapping), into a bare ``$ref`` mapping, or with the\nintermediate mappings the override lacks; the step 1 rules for new keys in that mapping apply\n(insertion point, sibling styles, block or flow). A key below a plain mapping is written as before.\n\nEach case loads a real theatre configuration (root file and included files on disk), resolves the\nset with the real TargetResolver, plans the patch, compares the patched files byte-exactly (the\nincluded file is never among them) and re-loads the patched texts with the real loader to prove\nthey read back as the intended configuration, in merged order.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Mapping\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import node_to_plain, plain_equal, root_plain\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_patch\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import AddKeyTarget, resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\n\nROOT = \"troupe.yml\"\nCODER = \"model: y\\ntools: 1\\n\"\nCODER_PARAMS = \"model: y\\nparams:\\n  t: 1\\n\"\n\n\ndef _plan(tmp_path: Path, files: Mapping[str, str], path: str, value: str) -> dict[str, str]:\n    \"\"\"Plan an add-key set; return each patched file's name and new text.\n\n    Also proves that the target is a new key, that every patch changes its file and that the\n    patched texts, re-loaded together, are the intended configuration (including key order).\n    \"\"\"\n    fo...[truncated 9318 chars]

tool: Edit {"file_path": "/work/repo/src/troupe/config/writeback/planner.py", "new_string": "    def add_key(self, target: AddKeyTarget, value: NewValue) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Plan a new key at its placement, with the missing intermediate mappings (3.1, 3.2).\n\n        A placement naming a written mapping (an override side, a ``$ref`` mapping or a plain\n        mapping) receives the keys ``prefix + missing`` there (config-include-overrides 4.1,\n        4.3-4.6). A scalar-form include to convert is written by the step 1 rule, into the\n        deepest existing mapping ``target.mapping`` with the keys ``missing``.\n\n        The first key is the new entry; the keys after it become nested new mappings with VALUE\n        at the last one, rendered as one new collection value (7.4).\n        \"\"\"\n        placement = target.placement\n        if placement.mapping is not None:\n            mapping = placement.mapping\n            keys = (*placement.prefix, *target.missing)\n        else:\n            mapping = target.mapping\n            keys = target.missing\n        first, *intermediates = keys\n        for key in reversed(intermediates):\n            value = NewMapping(entries=((key, value),))\n        self.add_entries(mapping, ((first, value),), self._path)", "old_string": "    def add_key(self, target: AddKeyTarget, value: NewValue) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Plan a new key in ``target.mapping``, with the missing intermediate mappings (3.1, 3.2).\n\n        The first missing key is the new entry; the keys after it become nested new mappings with\n        VALUE at the last one, rendered as one new collection value (7.4).\n        \"\"\"\n        first, *intermediates = target.missing\n        for key in reversed(intermediates):\n            value = NewMapping(entries=((key, value),))\n        self.add_entries(target.mapping, ((first, value),), self._path)", "replace_all": false}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/t.py <<'EOF'\nimport tempfile, pathlib\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_patch\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nd=pathlib.Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()); (d/\"troupe.yml\").write_text(\"agents:\\n  coder: {model: z}  # note\\n\")\ndoc=load_config(d/\"troupe.yml\"); p=ConfigPath.parse(\"agents.coder.temp\")\nprint(repr(plan_patch(doc, resolve_target(doc,p,parse_value(\"0.5\",p)))[0].new_text))\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/t.py; grep -n \"comment\" src/troupe/config/writeback/edits.py | head -20"}

The slot is\n        in that entry's file; ``target.node`` (the merged value) drives comparisons and kind\n        decisions, while the slot holds the written value (the override's own mapping when a\n        mapping present in both layers is replaced).\n        \"\"\"\n        parent = target.parent\n        if isinstance(parent, ConfigMapping):\n            written, entry = physical_entry(parent, str(target.key))\n            file = written.origin.file\n        else:\n            file = parent.origin.file\n        layout = self._guard(file, self._path, lambda: self._layout(file))\n\n    ...[truncated 473 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/probe.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys, tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import plain_equal, root_plain\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_patch\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\ncases = [\n (\"coder:\\n  $ref: file:coder.yaml\\n  params:\\n    u: 3\\n\", \"model: y\\nparams:\\n  t: 1\\n\", \"coder.params\", v)\n for v in [\"{}\", \"{u: 4, t: 1}\", \"{u: 3, t: 2}\", \"{u: 3, t: 1, k: 9}\", \"{t: 1}\", \"{u: 3}\", \"{u: 5}\"]\n] + [(\"coder: {$ref: file:coder.yaml, params: {u: 3}}\\n\", \"model: y\\nparams:\\n  t: 1\\n\", \"coder.params\", \"{t: 1}\")]\nfor root, inc, path, val in cases:\n    d = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()); (d/\"troupe.yml\").write_text(root); (d/\"coder.yaml\").write_text(inc)\n    doc = load_config(d/\"troupe.yml\"); p = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    req = resolve_target(doc, p, parse_value(val, p))\n    try:\n        patches = plan_patch(doc, req)\n        rel = load_config(d/\"troupe.yml\", {x.file.real_path: x.new_text for x in patches})\n        print(val, \"->\", {x.file.path.name: x.new_text for x in patches}, \"readback ok:\", plain_equal(root_plain(rel), req.intended, ordered=True))\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(val, \"-> ERR\", type(e).__name__, e)\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/probe.py"}
tool: Edit {"file_path": "/work/repo/src/troupe/config/writeback/planner.py", "new_string": "        preferred: QuoteStyle | None = None\n        if isinstance(node, ConfigMapping) and node.layers is not None:\n            if isinstance(value, NewMapping):\n                # Kept, removed and new keys of a merged mapping live in several files; replacing\n                # only the override would leave the included keys in the merged value.\n                _refuse(\n                    path,\n                    layout.file,\n                    \"this change cannot be written: a mapping merged from an include and its \"\n                    \"overrides cannot be replaced by a mapping\",\n                )\n            # Another kind replaces the override's own value only; the merged value is then it.\n            self._replace_value(layout, slot, value, path, preferred)\n            return True\n        if isinstance(node, ConfigScalar) and isinstance(value, NewScalar):", "old_string": "        preferred: QuoteStyle | None = None\n        if isinstance(node, ConfigScalar) and isinstance(value, NewScalar):", "replace_all": false}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat >> tests/writeback/test_planner_overrides.py <<'EOF'\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"value\", [\"{}\", \"{t: 1}\", \"{u: 4, t: 1}\"])\ndef test_merged_mapping_replaced_by_a_mapping_is_refused_for_now(tmp_path: Path, value: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 7.1, 7.3: a mapping present in both layers is not replaced by a mapping through the\n    override alone (that would keep the included keys); until the layered merge exists such a\n    change is refused with exit 3, naming PATH and the file of the override.\"\"\"\n    root = \"coder:\\n  $ref: file:coder.yaml\\n  params:\\n    u: 3\\n\"\n    error = _refused_replace(tmp_path, {ROOT: root, \"coder.yaml\": CODER_PARAMS}, \"coder.params\", value)\n    assert error.file == tmp_path / ROOT\n    assert error.path == \"coder.params\"\n    assert \"cannot be replaced by a mapping\" in str(error)\nEOF\nuv run ruff format tests/writeback/test_planner_overrides.py src/troupe/config/writeback/planner.py; uv run pytest tests/writeback/test_planner_overrides.py -q 2>&1 | tail -3; uv run python /tmp/probe.py"}

Keep the original task context (3.7, Requirements 4.5, 7.3, 7.4; boundary planner.py + tests/writeback/test_planner_overrides.py; do not edit tasks.md; do not commit). Fix:\n\nFinding 1 (Important, Product Principle 2): a block mapping that loses every entry, gains none and is not pruned is left as null (`params:`) — read-back misses it when the override hides the null base. Repros (real CLI): (a) root `agents.coder: {$ref: file:coder.yaml, params: {b: 20}}`, coder.yaml `params:\\n  a: 1\\n`; `set agents.coder.params '{n: 7}'` → coder.yaml becomes `params:` (null). (b) root `coder: file:coder.yaml`, coder.yaml `params:\\n  t: 1\\n`; `set agents.coder.params '{k: 2}'`-style (unmerged `_merge_mapping` with placement elsewhere) → coder.yaml `params:` null. Remediation: in `_flush_removals`, for a recorded removal that is not pruned, covers every entry of its mapping, and whose mapping receives no new entries, replace the mapping's value with `{}` at its holder's slot (as `_empty_layered` does) instead of deleting lines; same for the unmerged path in `_merge_mapping` when a block mapping loses every entry while its new keys are placed elsewhere. If the slot cannot be found (e.g. the top of an included file), refuse with exit 3 naming PATH. Add exact-text planner tests for both repros (included file becomes `params: {}`; for the scalar include case the conversion adds the override in the including file).\nFinding 2 (Suggestion, Principle 1 — please fix): new keys take sibling styles from an entry that pruning later deletes (repro: override `params:\\n  'inner':\\n    b: 2\\n`, coder.yaml `params:\\n  inner:\\n    a: 1\\n`; `set agents.coder.params '{inner: {a: 1}, new: 5}'` writes `'new': 5`). Take sibling styles after pruning is known, or exclude pruning-candidate entries from the sibling choice; add a test.\nInclude RED_PHASE_OUTPUT for the new tests ...[truncated 321 chars]

I'll continue once it reports back.

Nothing is committed and `tasks.md` is untouched.\n\nThe flow form of repro (a) was worse than reported. It now writes correctly.\n\nThe `{}` choice for a merged mapping replaced by an empty mapping is unchanged from round 0. It still needs recording in research.md or the tasks.md Implementation Notes.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 3.7\n- TASK_BRIEF: Round 1 fixes. (1) A written mapping that loses every entry, gains none and is not pruned is written as `{}` at its written entry, never left as null; this applies to the layered path and to the unmerged path whose new keys go elsewhere. It is refused with exit 3 naming PATH when no written entry holds it. A flow override that loses every entry but receives new ones is rendered anew with them. (2) New keys take sibling styles only from entries that remain after pruning. Conversions and the unmerged node's own insertion stay immediate. Same-index insertions keep planner order. (2) The refusal for an emptied mapping that no written entry holds ("has no written entry to hold an empty mapping") has no test. I found no input that reaches it, because the top of an included file is an include root and is refused earlier. (3) The `{}`-over-merged-mapping decision from round 0 still needs recording in research.md or tasks.md (outside my boundary).\n- EVIDENCE: All in planner.py. `_place` records `_additions` per written mapping and marks `_receivers`. `_flush_removals` works in three steps:\n  - It prunes upward first, as before.\n  - Each receiving mapping then gets one `add_entries` with `self._removed(mapping)`, which includes pruned entries. tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; git status --short; uv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_planner_overrides.py 2>&1|tail -1; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='spec/spec/config-include-overrides/research.md'\ns=open(p).read()\na=\"\\n### Product owner review (2026-10-05)\"\nassert a in s\ns=s.replace(a,'''\n### Decision 9: A mapping that loses every key becomes `{}`, never null (found in implementation, task 3.7)\n- **Context**: in a collection replacement, a written mapping (often in the included file) can lose every entry\n  and gain none. For example, VALUE replaces a merged mapping, or `{}` is placed over one. Deleting its lines\n  would leave `key:`, which is null. The read-back check can miss that when an override hides the null.\n- **Selected Approach**:\n  - A nested override that empties is pruned (Decision 4).\n  - Any other written mapping that empties is rewritten as `{}` at its written entry.\n  - `{}` placed over a merged mapping prunes the override and writes `{}` on the included side.\n  - When no written entry can hold the `{}`, the set is refused with exit 3.\n- **Alternatives**: refuse with exit 3 (safe but rejects valid requests); leave null (changes the type of the\n  included value, which nobody asked for).\n- **Rationale**: the file keeps the requested type, and the result matches the flow-style behaviour (step 1 writes\n  `{}` for an emptied flow mapping).\n- **Revisit**: if removals must never touch shared files.\n'''+a,1)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nT=spec/spec/config-include-overrides/tasks.md; sed -i 's/^- \\[ \\] 3\\.7 Merge/- [x] 3.7 Merge/' $T\necho '- 3.7: removals and additions into written mappings are deferred to `_flush_removals` (start of `patches()`), so pruning and sibling styles see the final picture; emptied non-prunable mappings become `{}` (research Decision 9).
