tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Unit tests of ``set --in-include`` in the file layer (config-include-overrides task 3.8, CR-B).\n\nWith ``in_include`` the target resolver skips the placement walk and uses the step 1 rule: a new\nkey (and each new key of a mapping VALUE) goes into the included side of the deepest existing\nancestor, followed through nested merged includes to the innermost file, or into that ancestor\nitself when it is not merged. Existing values, appends and removals do not depend on the choice.\nWhen keys go into the included side of a merged mapping, the intended configuration lists them\nafter the included keys and before the override-only keys, so the ordered read-back holds\n(design.md \"Placement with --in-include\", research.md Decision 8).\n\nEach case loads a real theatre configuration from disk, resolves the set with the real\nTargetResolver, plans the patch, compares the patched files byte-exactly and re-loads the patched\ntexts with the real loader to prove they read back as the intended configuration, in merged\norder (the merged order of the changed mapping is also checked explicitly after reload).\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Mapping\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import (\n    IncludeDirective,\n    PlainMapping,\n    PlainValue,\n    plain_equal,\n    root_plain,\n)\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_patch\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\n\nROOT = \"troupe.yml\"\nCODER = \"model: y\\ntools: 1\\n\"\nCODER_PARAMS = \"model: y\\nparams:\\n  t: 1\\n\"\n\n\ndef _plan(\n    tmp_path: Path, files: Mapping[str, str], path: str, value: str, *, in_include: bool = True\n) -> tuple[dict[str, str], PlainMapping]:\n    \"\...[truncated 9599 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"New keys below includes, end to end through the real ``troupe config set`` (config-include-overrides 4.1).\n\nA new key whose deepest existing ancestor comes from an included file is written as an override in\nthe file holding that include's reference (4.1-4.6); a scalar-form include is first converted to\nmapping form (5.1-5.5). tool: Bash {"command": "mkdir -p /tmp/s42 && cat > /tmp/s42/try.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys, shutil, json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nsys.path.insert(0, \"/work/repo/tests\")\nfrom support import write_theatre\nfrom set_support import run_set, run_config, snapshot_tree\n\nCODER = \"# shared coder settings\\nmodel: small\\n\\nmax_tokens: 100  # cap\\n\"\ndef case(name, root, files, path, value):\n    d = Path(\"/tmp/s42\")/name\n    shutil.rmtree(d, ignore_errors=True)\n    write_theatre(d, root, files=files)\n    run_config(\"show\", \"--theatre\", str(d), cwd=d)\n    before = snapshot_tree(d)\n    r = run_set(d, path, value)\n    after = snapshot_tree(d)\n    print(f\"=== {name}: set {path} {value!r} -> exit {r.exit_code} out={r.stdout!r} err={r.stderr!r}\")\n    for k in sorted(set(before)|set(after)):\n        if before.get(k) != after.get(k):\n            print(f\"--- {k} changed:\\n{(after.get(k) or b'<gone>').decode()}\")\n    h = run_config(\"history\", \"--theatre\", str(d), cwd=d).stdout.decode().splitlines()\n    for l in h:\n        j = json.loads(l)\n        if j[\"op\"] != \"import\": print(\"   ev\", j[\"op\"], j[\"path\"], j[\"old\"], j[\"new\"], j[\"via\"])\n    s = run_config(\"show\", \"--theatre\", str(d), cwd=d)\n    print(\"show:\", s.exit_code, s.stdout.decode(), s.stderr)\n\nexec(open(sys.argv[1]).read())\nEOF\ncat > /tmp/s42/c1.py <<'EOF'\nROOT = \"agents:\\n  coder: file:agents/coder.yaml  # c\\n\"\nTOOLS = \"model: small\\ntools:  # list\\n  - file:agents/coder.yaml\\n  - lead\\n\"\ncase(\"63item\", LROOT, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": CODER}, \"agents.0.temperature\", \"0.3\")\nLROOTC = \"name: demo\\n...[truncated 229 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cat > /tmp/s42/c2.py <<'EOF'\nOV = \"agents:\\n  coder:  # c\\n    $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n    model: big  # mine\\n  lead: x\\n\"\nTOOLS = \"model: small\\ntools:  # list\\n  - read\\n  - write\\n\"\ncase(\"61append_merged\", OV, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": TOOLS}, \"agents.coder.tools.2\", \"grep\")\nOVL = \"agents:\\n  coder:\\n    $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n    tools: [read]\\n\"\ncase(\"61append_override\", OVL, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": TOOLS}, \"agents.coder.tools.1\", \"grep\")\nCODER = \"# shared coder settings\\nmodel: small\\n\\nmax_tokens: 100  # cap\\n\"\ncase(\"71\", OV, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": CODER}, \"agents.coder.model\", \"huge\")\ncase(\"72\", OV, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": CODER}, \"agents.coder.max_tokens\", \"200\")\nNC = \"model: small\\nparams:  # tuning\\n  top_k: 40\\n  seed: 7\\nmax_tokens: 100\\n\"\nOVP = \"agents:\\n  coder:\\n    $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n    params:\\n      seed: 9  # mine\\n      temp: 0.3\\n    model: big\\n\"\ncase(\"73\", OVP, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": NC}, \"agents.coder.params\", \"{top_k: 50, seed: 9, top_p: 0.9}\")\ncase(\"74both\", OVP, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": NC}, \"agents.coder.params\", \"{top_k: 40, temp: 0.3}\")\ncase(\"74prune\", OVP, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": NC}, \"agents.coder.params\", \"{top_k: 40}\")\ncase(\"74empty\", OVP, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": NC}, \"agents.coder.params\", \"{}\")\ncase(\"73whole\", OVP, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": NC}, \"agents.coder\", \"{model: big, params: {top_k: 40, seed: 9, temp: 0.3}}\")\nEOF\ncd /work/repo && uv run python /tmp/s42/try.py /tmp/s42/c2.py"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cat > /tmp/s42/c3.py <<'EOF'\nCODER = \"# shared coder settings\\nmodel: small\\n\\nmax_tokens: 100  # cap\\n\"\nT = \"agents:\\n  coder:\\n    $ref: file:prompt.md  # bare\\n  lead: x\\n\"\ncase(\"76\", T, {\".troupe/prompt.md\": \"You write code.\\n\"}, \"agents.coder\", \"You review code.\")\ncase(\"76flow\", \"agents:\\n  coder: {$ref: file:prompt.md}\\n\", {\".troupe/prompt.md\": \"You write code.\\n\"}, \"agents.coder\", \"You review code.\")\nOV = \"agents:\\n  coder:  # c\\n    $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n    model: big  # mine\\n  lead: x\\n\"\ncase(\"81\", OV, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": CODER}, \"agents.coder.$ref\", \"file:other.yaml\")\ncase(\"81new\", OV, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": CODER}, \"agents.lead2.$ref\", \"x\")\nR = \"name: demo\\nagents:\\n  lead: x\\n\"\ncase(\"82\", R, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": CODER}, \"agents.coder\", \"{$ref: file:agents/coder.yaml, temperature: 0.3}\")\ncase(\"82missing\", R, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": CODER}, \"agents.coder\", \"{$ref: file:agents/missing.yaml, temperature: 0.3}\")\ncase(\"82notstr\", R, {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": CODER}, \"agents.coder\", \"{$ref: 7, temperature: 0.3}\")\ncase(\"82replace\", \"name: demo\\nagents:\\n  coder: x  # old\\n\", {\".troupe/agents/coder.yaml\": CODER}, \"agents.coder\", \"{$ref: file:agents/coder.yaml, temperature: 0.3}\")\nEOF\ncd /work/repo && uv run python /tmp/s42/try.py /tmp/s42/c3.py; ls -la /tmp/s42/82missing/.troupe /tmp/s42/82missing/.troupe/agents"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/s42/sab.sh <<'EOF'\n#!/bin/bash\n# usage: sab.sh NAME FILE PYOLD PYNEW -k EXPR\nname=$1; file=$2; old=$3; new=$4; k=$5\npython3 - \"$file\" \"$old\" \"$new\" <<'PY'\nimport sys\np,o,n=sys.argv[1:4]\ns=open(p).read(); assert o in s, o; open(p,'w').write(s.replace(o,n,1))\nPY\necho \"##### $name\"\nuv run pytest tests/test_set_overrides.py -q -n 4 -k \"$k\" 2>&1 | grep -E \"^(FAILED|ERROR)|passed|failed\" | sed 's/ - .\\{0,160\\}/&/' | cut -c1-260\ngit checkout -- \"$file\"\nEOF\nchmod +x /tmp/s42/sab.sh\nP=src/troupe/config/writeback/target.py\n/tmp/s42/sab.sh \"S1 6.2 list item does not stop the placement walk\" $P \"        if isinstance(link.parent, ConfigSequence):\n            break\" \"        pass\" \"included_list or list_item\"\n/tmp/s42/sab.sh \"S2 6.3 scalar-form include not converted\" $P \"        if include is not None and include.kind is IncludeKind.YAML and link.parent is not None:\" \"        if False:\" \"list_item_include\"\n/tmp/s42/sab.sh \"S3 7.x physical entry base-first\" src/troupe/config/overrides.py \"    if _override(layers.over, key) is not None:\n        return physical_entry(layers.over, key)\" \"    if layers.base.get(key) is None and _override(layers.over, key) is not None:\n        return physical_entry(layers.over, key)\" \"overridden or not_overridden or replacement or emptied or every_key\"\n/tmp/s42/sab.sh \"S8 6.1 append writes twice\" src/troupe/config/writeback/planner.py \"self.add_entries(target.sequence, ((None, value),), self._path)\" \"self.add_entries(target.sequence, ((None, value), (None, value)), self._path)\" \"append\"\ngit status --short"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/rev44b.py <<'EOF'\nimport re,ast,pathlib,subprocess\nreq=pathlib.Path(\"spec/spec/config-include-overrides/requirements.md\").read_text()\nids=set();cur=None\nfor line in req.splitlines():\n    m=re.match(r\"### Requirement (\\d+)\",line)\n    if m: cur=m.group(1);continue\n    m=re.match(r\"(\\d+)\\. \",line)\n    if cur and m: ids.add(f\"{cur}.{m.group(1)}\")\nF=[\"tests/test_set_overrides.py\",\"tests/test_set_in_include.py\",\"tests/test_e2e_overrides_read.py\",\"tests/test_e2e_overrides_refusals.py\",\"tests/test_overrides_merge.py\",\"tests/test_loader_overrides.py\",\"tests/writeback/test_in_include.py\",\"tests/writeback/test_planner_overrides.py\"]\nnamed=set()\nfor f in F:\n    for n in ast.walk(ast.parse(open(f).read())):\n        if isinstance(n,ast.FunctionDef) and n.name.startswith(\"test\"):\n            d=ast.get_docstring(n) or \"\"\n            if not d.startswith(\"Req\"): continue\n            for a,b,c in re.findall(r\"(\\d+)\\.(\\d+)(?:-(\\d+))?\",d.split(\":\")[0]):\n                for k in range(int(b),int(c or b)+1): named.add(f\"{a}.{k}\")\nprint(len(ids),\"ids; missing\",sorted(ids-named),\"phantom\",sorted(named-ids))\ndef strip(t):\n    tree=ast.parse(t)\n    for n in ast.walk(tree):\n        if isinstance(n,(ast.Module,ast.FunctionDef,ast.ClassDef)) and n.body and isinstance(n.body[0],ast.Expr) and isinstance(n.body[0].value,ast.Constant): n.body[0].value.value=\"\"\n    return ast.dump(tree)\nfor f in subprocess.check_output([\"git\",\"diff\",\"--name-only\",\"--\",\"src\",\"tests\"],text=True).split():\n    print(f, strip(subprocess.check_output([\"git\",\"show\",\"HEAD:\"+f],text=True))==strip(open(f).read()))\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/rev44b.py; grep -c \"def test_own_include\" tests/test_set_overrides.py; git diff -- tests | grep -c '^+.*def test_'"}

assistant: The feature is mostly sound, but it is not ready for GO. I found one Important defect: a valid `set` is refused with exit 3 when one mapping VALUE adds keys at two sibling places that both need new override mappings. It refuses safely (no file changed) but breaks Req 4.4 and 7.3. There is also one open Implementation Note that needs a product owner decision.\n\nThe full suite passes: pytest 3148 passed (8 min), `mypy --strict` clean, `ruff check` and `ruff format --check` clean. I modified no files.\n\n**E. Cross-task integration**\n\nThe interfaces match from loader to CLI. `KeyPlacement`, `IncludeSite`, `Holder`/`holder_of` and `SetRequest.in_include` are used the same way by target, planner and setter. `place_new_keys` is the single placement routine, used both for new keys and for collection replacements. `convert_include` is the single conversion path. Removals and additions are held back in `_flush_removals` (called at the start of `patches()`), so pruning and sibling styles see the final state.\n\nI ran these combinations through the real CLI in temporary theatres and checked the bytes and history:\n- `--in-include` with a mapping VALUE that removes one key from both layers and one from the included side, and adds a key. The add went into the included file, the removals were made in both files, and history shows one set and two removes with the reason.\n- Pruning an emptied override, and `{}` over a merged mapping: the override was pruned and `params: {}` was written in the included file.\n- Nested includes: block conversion in the middle file keeping `params:   # pp`, a compact list-item conversion, a flow `{$ref: …, timeout: 6}`, and a quoted `"file:…"` kept verbatim. `show --sources` labels were correct and there was one set event each.\n- A root file that is a `$ref`: new keys go to the override, `--in-include` writes into the base file in the right merged order, a bare root gains overrides, and a `$ref` in PATH gives exit 3.\n- `watch` after a converting `set`: edits to the target and the override were recorded with `via file`. Rewriting the override between block and flow form recorded nothing.\n- An override value that is itself an include (`params: file:p.yaml`): it was converted in place, and `--in-include` and the removal/pruning cases were correct.\n- A VALUE with `$ref` round-trips; a missing target gives exit 3. `get …$ref` gives exit 5. `--in-include=x` gives exit 2.\n\nFindings:\n1. **Important (LOCAL)** – new keys sharing a prefix are not merged before writing. One VALUE that adds keys below two sibling mappings, where the override lacks both, is refused. For example `set agents.coder.params '{a:{x:1,n1:1}, b:{y:2,n2:2}}'`:\n   - Scalar-form include: exit 3 with `internal error: overlapping edits at index 16`, because `convert_include` is called twice on the same include.\n   - Existing `$ref` mapping: exit 3 with `duplicate key in mapping`, because two `params:` prefixes are appended into the same override.\n   - The same request succeeds once `params` already exists in the override. Fix: in `_Planner._place` / `_additions`, group placements by mapping or include and merge the `_nest(prefix, …)` trees before rendering. Add a planner test and an e2e test.\n2. **Warning (UPSTREAM)** – the placement order disagrees with the product owner's choice 5 when an included file's root is itself a bare `$ref`. With `troupe.yml: c: {$ref: file:a.yaml, x: 1}`, `a.yaml: $ref: file:b.yaml` and `sub` in `b.yaml`, `set c.sub.n 2` writes into `troupe.yml`. Choice 5 ("the file holding the reference to the include the ancestor's content comes from") points to `a.yaml`. Both includes sit at the same node, so "nearest" is ambiguous; the walk in design.md checks the merged mapping first. No placement test pins this case. A spec clarification is needed.\n3. **Warning (LOCAL)** – stale comment at `src/troupe/config/model.py:159`: `MergeLayers.base` says "an include root's base has include=None", but since commit 14ec53f the loader keeps the target root's own `$ref` include, as design.md says.\n\n**F. Requirements coverage**\n\nEvery ID from 1.1 to 10.7 is named in at least one test docstring; 1.3, 3.4, 3.5 and 7.6 have the fewest, at 2 each. The traceability table in design.md is complete. Coverage gaps:\n- Finding 1 is a behaviour gap against 4.4 and 7.3 for multi-prefix VALUEs.\n- No test for finding 2's chained bare root.\n\n**G. Design alignment**\n\n- **Dependency direction holds.** `overrides.py` imports only `constants` and `model`, nothing from writeback. The loader imports overrides. `layout.py` uses `written_parts`. The planner uses `physical_entry`/`physical_holders`/`holder_of`. `verify` and `target` use `root_plain`. The CLI touches core only through `set_value(..., in_include=)`.\n- **FYI (LOCAL), drift:** `target.py` imports only `is_ref_key` from overrides. The design graph shows Target depending on Overrides for lookups, but it walks `layers` directly.\n- **FYI (LOCAL), test files differ from the File Structure Plan:**\n  - Extra files: `tests/test_overrides_merge.py`, `tests/test_e2e_overrides_refusals.py`, `tests/writeback/test_in_include.py`.\n  - Also touched: `tests/writeback/test_collection_render.py`, `tests/writeback/test_verify.py`, `tests/writeback/test_planner_add.py`.\n  - All are sensible; the plan just doesn't list them.\n- **FYI (LOCAL):** `overrides.py` adds `holder_of`, `Layer`/`LayerStep` and `Holder.is_override`, which design.md does not list. These are the pruning support Implementation Note 1.1 anticipated.\n\n**G.5 Boundary audit** (`git diff 963fb1c..HEAD --stat -- src`)\n\n- `reconcile.py`, `resolve.py`, `watch.py` and `cli/yaml_output.py` changed docstrings only, which is within bounds. The `yaml_output.py` docstring edit technically falls outside "only `set_command.py` changes", but it is harmless (FYI).\n- `collection_render.py` gained only `render_ref_form_lines` and `render_flow_ref_form`, with no change to existing rendering rules. The history, lineage and reconcile code is untouched, and there is no new dependency.\n- **FYI (LOCAL):** `spec.json` still has `phase: "tasks-generated"`, not updated after implementation.\n\n**H. Implementation Notes**\n\n- **3.2 leniency – Important (UPSTREAM, needs a product owner decision).** I reproduced it. Over `coder: {$ref: file:agents/coder.yaml, temperature: 0.5}`, both `set agents.coder 'file:agents/coder.yaml'` and `set agents.coder '{$ref: file:agents/coder.yaml}'` exit 0 and change nothing, with no event. A user would expect the overrides to be removed, or else a refusal: the merged value would change, which reads as "differs" under 7.5. The options are to make a contentless directive differ from an include with overrides (exit 3), or to support removing overrides. Either way the decision should go into requirements 7.5 / 8.2.\n- **3.5 – FYI.** A list item written with extra spaces after `-` cannot be converted (exit 3), the same as step 1 behaves. Acceptable.\n- **3.6 – resolved.** The interim refusal was removed in 3.7, which I confirmed with the merged-mapping replacement runs.\n- **3.7 / Decision 9 – FYI.** An emptied written mapping becomes `{}` and an override is pruned. Both worked in the CLI runs. Note that removals do write into the shared file, which Decision 9 lists as a revisit trigger.\n- **4.2 / 7.5 – FYI.** A mapping equal to the merged value is refused with exit 3 by design; this is pinned by a test and confirmed in the CLI runs.\n\n**Verdict:** NO-GO until finding 1 is fixed. Findings 2 and H-3.2 need product owner clarification; the rest are cosmetic.
assistant: The validator returned NO-GO with one real defect. When a single `set` adds keys under two sibling sections that both need new override sections, it is wrongly refused with exit 3. No file is changed, but the request should have succeeded.\n\nIt also found a corner case where the code doesn't match your confirmed choice 5: an included file whose root is itself a `$ref` to a third file. In that case the new key should go in the middle file, but it currently lands in `troupe.yml`. I'll fix both, plus a stale model comment, then re-validate.

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat >> tests/test_set_overrides.py <<'EOF'\n\n\n# ---- 4.4, 7.3: new keys sharing a placement are merged (validate-impl remediation) ----\n\nSIBLINGS_CODER = \"params:\\n  a:\\n    x: 1\\n  b:\\n    y: 2\\n\"\nSIBLINGS_ADDED = \"    params:\\n      a:\\n        n1: 1\\n      b:\\n        n2: 2\\n\"\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    \"root\",\n    [\n        \"agents:\\n  coder: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n\",\n        \"agents:\\n  coder:\\n    $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n    model: z\\n\",\n    ],\n    ids=[\"scalar-form\", \"ref-mapping\"],\n)\ndef test_new_keys_below_two_included_siblings_are_written_as_one_override(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre, root: str\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 4.4, 7.3, 4.7, 5.1, 9.1, 9.3: `set agents.coder.params {a: {x: 1, n1: 1}, b: {y: 2,\n    n2: 2}}`, where `a` and `b` come from coder.yaml only, writes one `params` override holding\n    both new keys (the scalar-form include converted once, or one entry in the `$ref` mapping);\n    coder.yaml is unchanged; two `set` events in one change.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(root, files={CODER_FILE: SIBLINGS_CODER})\n    start = _imported(theatre)\n    before = snapshot_tree(theatre)\n\n    result = run_set(theatre, \"agents.coder.params\", \"{a: {x: 1, n1: 1}, b: {y: 2, n2: 2}}\")\n\n    _assert_success(result)\n    converted = \"agents:\\n  coder:\\n    $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n\"\n    expected = (converted if root.endswith(\"coder.yaml\\n\") else root) + SIBLINGS_ADDED\n    _assert_only_changed(theatre, before, ROOT, expected)\n    _assert_set_events(\n        theatre,\n        start,\n        [\n            (\"set\", \"agents.coder.params.a.n1\", None, 1, \"cli\"),\n            (\"set\", \"agents.coder.params.b.n2\", None, 2, \"cli\"),\n        ],\n    )\n    assert _show(theatre, \"agents.coder.params\") == b\"a:\\n  x: 1\\n  n1: 1\\nb:\\n  y: 2\\n  n2: 2\\n\"\n\n\n# ---- choice 5: an override on a file whose root is itself a `$ref` mapping ----\...[truncated 1614 chars]

A == X, or A written in the override, still writes into X's override (4.3).\n\n### Decision 3'''\nassert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new,1)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n \"one insertion\\|routed additions\\|conversion\" spec/spec/config-include-overrides/design.md | head -20"}

tool: Bash {"command": "mkdir -p /tmp/rv && cat > /tmp/rv/t.sh <<'EOF'\n#!/bin/bash\n# usage: t.sh name ; reads files from env-defined heredocs beforehand\nEOF\ncat > /tmp/rv/run.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys, subprocess, shutil, os, json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nTROUPE = \"/work/repo/.venv/bin/troupe\"\ndef case(name, files, *setargs):\n    root = Path(\"/tmp/rv\")/name\n    shutil.rmtree(root, ignore_errors=True)\n    for rel, c in files.items():\n        p = root/\".troupe\"/rel; p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True); p.write_text(c)\n    subprocess.run([TROUPE,\"config\",\"show\",\"--theatre\",str(root)],capture_output=True)\n    before = {p: p.read_text() for p in (root/\".troupe\").rglob(\"*.y*ml\")}\n    r = subprocess.run([TROUPE,\"config\",\"set\",*setargs,\"--theatre\",str(root)],capture_output=True,text=True)\n    print(f\"=== {name}: set {setargs} -> exit {r.returncode}\")\n    if r.stdout.strip(): print(\"stdout:\", r.stdout.strip())\n    if r.stderr.strip(): print(\"stderr:\", r.stderr.strip())\n    for p in sorted(before):\n        now = p.read_text()\n        if now != before[p]:\n            print(f\"--- CHANGED {p.relative_to(root)}:\\n{now}\", end=\"\")\n    s = subprocess.run([TROUPE,\"config\",\"show\",\"--theatre\",str(root)],capture_output=True,text=True)\n    print(\"--- show:\\n\"+s.stdout, s.stderr)\nexec(open(sys.argv[1]).read())\nEOF\nls /work/repo/.venv/bin/troupe; cat /tmp/rv_mech.txt"}

tool: SendMessage {"content": "Remediation round 1 review: the planner merge fix…", "message": "Remediation round 1 review: the planner merge fix (finding 1) is APPROVED as is; the choice-5 fix (finding 2) is REJECTED as partial. Same context/rules as before (do not edit tasks.md or spec.json, do not commit). The current branch only fires when the outer include has overrides; bare outer (`c:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n`), scalar outer (`c: file:a.yaml`), and a scalar middle root (a.yaml = `file:b.yaml`) still write into troupe.yml; and troupe `c: {$ref: file:a.yaml, sub: {k: 1}}`, a.yaml `$ref: file:b.yaml`, b.yaml `sub: {deep: {m: 1}}`, `set c.sub.deep.n 2` writes into troupe's `sub` override instead of a.yaml.\n\nReplace the walk in target.py `_place` (non-in_include path) with this rule, based on the layers that actually hold A's content (A = deepest existing mapping):\n1. Build the holder chain from the root along PATH's existing keys: for each node on the way append it; when a node N is merged (layers), append N, then continue with the side that holds the next key — N.layers.over if the key is written there, else N.layers.base — appending that side (recursively: a side may itself be merged; apply the same choice), and only then take the next key's value. For A itself: if A is merged, append A then its over side (A written in an override → Req 4.3).\n2. Walk the chain up from its end (innermost first). Stop at the first element that is:\n   a. a side reached as `over` of a merged node (content written in an override): place into the written mapping holding A's content (the last physical mapping of the chain), prefix () — Req 4.3;\n   b. a YAML include root (element.include set, kind YAML): mapping form (include.form set, bare or merged) → place into include.form (the `$ref` mapping) with prefix = keys from this element down to A; scalar form → convert this include (IncludeSite) with that prefix — this includes an include whose directive is a whole file root (a.yaml = `file:b.yaml`):...[truncated 1572 chars]

That covers bare, scalar and override forms, including a file whose root is itself an include. I'll re-review and re-validate when it reports back.
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/insp.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys, os, tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\ndef show(n, ind=0, label='root'):\n    from troupe.config.model import ConfigMapping\n    inc = n.include\n    i = f\" inc={inc.target.path.name}{' form@'+inc.form.origin.file.path.name if inc.form else ''} dir@{inc.directive.file.path.name}\" if inc else \"\"\n    print(' '*ind + f\"{label}: {type(n).__name__} file={n.origin.file.path.name}{i}{' MERGED' if getattr(n,'layers',None) else ''} id={id(n)%100000}\")\n    if isinstance(n, ConfigMapping):\n        if n.layers:\n            show(n.layers.base, ind+4, '[base]'); show(n.layers.over, ind+4, '[over]')\n        for e in n.entries:\n            if isinstance(e.value, ConfigMapping) or e.value.include: show(e.value, ind+2, e.key)\ncases = {\n 'bare': {'troupe.yml':'c:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n','a.yaml':'$ref: file:b.yaml\\n','b.yaml':'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'},\n 'scalar': {'troupe.yml':'c: file:a.yaml\\n','a.yaml':'$ref: file:b.yaml\\n','b.yaml':'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'},\n 'scalarmid': {'troupe.yml':'c:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n  x: 1\\n','a.yaml':'file:b.yaml\\n','b.yaml':'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'},\n 'deep': {'troupe.yml':'c:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n  sub:\\n    k: 1\\n','a.yaml':'$ref: file:b.yaml\\n','b.yaml':'sub:\\n  deep:\\n    m: 1\\n'},\n}\nfor name, files in cases.items():\n    d = tempfile.mkdtemp()\n    for f,t in files.items(): Path(d,f).write_text(t)\n    print('==', name)\n    try: show(load_config(Path(d,'troupe.yml')).root)\n    except Exception as e: print('ERR', e)\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/insp.py"}

tool: Edit {"file_path": "/work/repo/tests/writeback/test_target_placement.py", "new_string": "    placement = _placement(document, \"c.sub.n\")\n    assert placement.mapping is _override_side(_mapping_at(document, \"c.sub\"))\n    assert _file(tmp_path, placement.mapping) == Path(\"troupe.yml\")\n    assert placement.prefix == ()\n\n\ndef _ref_mapping_of(tmp_path: Path, placement: KeyPlacement, file: str) -> ConfigMapping:\n    \"\"\"The placement's mapping, asserted to be the `$ref` mapping written as the root of\n    ``file`` (its first entry is `$ref`, at the start of the file).\"\"\"\n    mapping = placement.mapping\n    assert mapping is not None\n    assert _file(tmp_path, mapping) == Path(file)\n    assert mapping.entries[0].key == \"$ref\"\n    assert mapping.origin.span.start == 0\n    return mapping\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    \"outer\",\n    [\"c:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n\", \"c: file:a.yaml\\n\", \"c: {$ref: file:a.yaml}\\n\"],\n    ids=[\"bare-outer\", \"scalar-outer\", \"flow-bare-outer\"],\n)\ndef test_key_from_a_chained_include_without_outer_overrides_goes_into_the_middle_file(\n    tmp_path: Path, outer: str\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 4.2, 4.4 (choice 5): whatever the form of troupe.yml's include of a.yaml (bare, scalar,\n    flow), when a.yaml's root is `$ref: file:b.yaml` and `c.sub` comes from b.yaml, `c.sub.n`\n    goes into a.yaml's `$ref` mapping with prefix `sub`; troupe.yml is not converted.\"\"\"\n    document = _load(tmp_path, outer, {\"a.yaml\": \"$ref: file:b.yaml\\n\", \"b.yaml\": CHAINED_INNER})\n    placement = _placement(document, \"c.sub.n\")\n    _ref_mapping_of(tmp_path, placement, \"a.yaml\")\n    assert placement.convert is None\n    assert placement.prefix == (\"sub\",)\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    \"outer\",\n    [CHAINED_ROOT, \"c:\\n  $ref: file:a.yaml\\n\", \"c: file:a.yaml\\n\"],\n    ids=[\"outer-with-overrides\", \"bare-outer\", \"scalar-outer\"],\n)\ndef test_key_from_a_scalar_middle_root_converts_the_middle_files_...[truncated 2539 chars]

assistant: The round 1 fixes hold: findings 1, 2 and 3 are resolved, and I found no new Critical or Important defect. The one open item is still H‑3.2 (a product owner decision). The new walk does turn one case that used to succeed into an exit 3, which needs a product owner call. I ran the real CLI in temporary theatres on `253f4b7`; I did not run the full suite, as agreed. I modified no files, and I removed my temporary theatres and the temporary worktree of `c351d18`.\n\n**Earlier findings, re-checked**\n1. **Finding 1 (multiple new keys under one placement) – resolved.**\n   - With a scalar-form include, `set agents.coder.params '{a:{x:1,n1:1}, b:{y:2,n2:2}}'` now gives one conversion with `params: a: n1 / b: n2`, and `coder.yaml` is unchanged.\n   - With an existing `$ref` mapping, adding three nested keys and removing `b.y` gives exit 0. `params` is written once, `b: {}` is left in the included file (Decision 9), and history is correct.\n   - Mixed removals, grouped additions and cascading pruning all came out right.\n2. **Finding 2 (choice 5, chained includes) – resolved.**\n   - `a.yaml = $ref: file:b.yaml`: the key goes into `a.yaml`.\n   - `a2.yaml = file:b2.yaml   # root inc`: the root is converted in place as `$ref: file:b2.yaml   # root inc` plus the entries.\n   - A key directly under the merged `c` still goes to `troupe.yml` (4.3). `show --sources` labels and events are correct, with `--reason` kept.\n3. **Finding 3 (stale comment in `model.py`) – resolved.**\n\n**E. Cross-task integration**\n- The interfaces line up. `IncludeSite` now allows `parent`/`key` to be None and has a `ref` property. `_Holder` replaces `_Link`. Conversions are grouped by the identity of the `IncludeRef` and run once in `_flush_removals`, before the additions.\n- New combinations all passed:\n  - **Lists:** a list-item include with a comment, an item already in `$ref` form, a chained item (written into `chain.yaml`), and in an included list both a nested flow-free conversion and the list-item stop (6.2).\n  - **`--in-include` on chained includes:** the key goes to the innermost file and the merged order is right.\n  - **Removals and additions in a chained `$ref` flow override:** the result is `sub: {z: 9}`.\n  - **Root file `file:x.yaml`:** handled when indented, quoted, with a comment, with CRLF, or with no final newline. `--- file:x.yaml` is refused with exit 3, as designed.\n  - **`watch`:** after a set that converted a chained middle root, edits to `b.yaml` and to the middle override were recorded with `via file`. A hand edit that leaves the merged value unchanged recorded nothing.\n- **Warning (UPSTREAM) – behaviour change, needs a product owner call.**\n  - **What changes:** a middle file shared by two includes now refuses new keys below it with exit 3. With `troupe.yml` = `a: file:mid.yaml`, `b: file:mid.yaml` and `mid.yaml` = `$ref: file:leaf.yaml` (or `file:leaf.yaml`), `set a.sub.n 1` used to succeed at `c351d18`, writing `a: {$ref: file:mid.yaml, sub: {n: 1}}` into `troupe.yml`. It now fails with exit 3.\n  - **Why:** this follows choice 5 literally and matches the existing nested key-level case (`co.yaml` = `p: file:leaf.yaml` shared → exit 3).\n  - **The concern:** it works against the step's goal of keeping shared includes shared.\n  - **What the user sees:** only the generic "written configuration differs from the intended configuration".\n  - **Coverage:** no test pins this refusal; tasks.md mentions it only in the Implementation Notes.\n  - **Product owner to decide:** accept as is, or improve the message (and possibly fall back to the outer file).\n- **FYI (UPSTREAM) – root file conversion.** A root file that is itself `file:x.yaml` now takes new keys as an override in the root file instead of in `x.yaml`. This is consistent with 4.1 and recorded in research.md.\n- **FYI – include roots inside a replacement.** A collection replacement that adds keys below an override value which is itself an include root (`t: file:t.yaml`) is refused with exit 3 under 2.5/7.5. This was already the behaviour before the fix.\n\n**F. Requirements coverage**\n- Every ID from 1.1 to 10.7 is still named in a test docstring. The 21 new tests name 4.1–4.5, 4.7, 5.1, 5.2, 5.5, 7.3, 9.1–9.3 and choice 5.\n- **Warning (LOCAL):** the only gap is the shared-middle refusal above, which no test covers.\n\n**G. Design alignment**\n- The "Placement walk" section of design.md was rewritten and now matches `target._holders` / `_walk` / `_into_form`. design.md also documents `IncludeRef.inner`, the grouping of placements and `_convert_root`.\n- Dependency direction is unchanged: `target.py` adds only `IncludeRef` from `model`, and `overrides.py`, `layout.py`, `compare.py` and the CLI are untouched. No new files were added.\n- **FYI (LOCAL):** `IncludeRef.inner` is a model field. Under the design's Revalidation Triggers, model field changes mean re-checking loader, layout, planner, target and compare; only the loader and target read it, and compare's docstring mentions it.\n\n**G.5 Boundary audit**\n- The fix touches only `loader.py`, `model.py`, `planner.py` and `target.py`, all owned by this spec. No rendering rules changed (`_convert_root` reuses `render_ref_form_lines`), and get/show/reconcile/watch and the CLI are unchanged.\n- **FYI (LOCAL):** `spec.json` still says `phase: "tasks-generated"`.\n\n**H. Implementation Notes**\n- **3.2 – Important (UPSTREAM), open, with the product owner.** Over an include that has overrides, `set PATH 'file:…'` or `'{$ref: file:…}'` exits 0 and changes nothing, with no event. Unchanged by this round.\n- **Round 1 note – Warning (UPSTREAM).** It records the shared-middle refusal; this is the product owner question above.\n- **3.5, 3.7 / Decision 9, 4.2 – FYI.** Unchanged and still behave as recorded: `{}` emptied mappings and pruning were re-checked in the CLI.\n\n**Verdict:** GO as far as the code goes. Before final acceptance the product owner should decide H‑3.2 and the shared-middle refusal, and a test should pin the shared-middle behaviour, whichever way it is decided.

Every command (`get`, `show --sources`, `history`, reconcile, `watch`) sees the merged result.\n  - `set` writes a new key under an included section as an override in the file holding the include reference. It converts `coder: file:agents/coder.yaml  # c` exactly as in the contract example.\n  - Existing values still change where they are written.\n- **CR-B:** `set --in-include PATH VALUE` writes the new key into the included file instead (the step-1 rule).\n\n**Fixed during implementation and validation**\n- The loader dropped an include when an included file's own root was a `$ref`.\n- Values with nested include overrides were wrongly refused.\n- A section emptied in the shared file was left as null; it is now written as `{}` (recorded as Decision 9).\n- A single `set` adding keys under two sibling sections was refused.\n- With chained includes, a new key landed in the outer file instead of the file you named in choice 5.\n\n**Behaviour changes worth knowing**\n- A root `troupe.yml` that is just `file:x.yaml` now gets new keys as `$ref: file:x.yaml` plus overrides in `troupe.yml`, not in `x.yaml`. This follows Req 4.1.\n- With chained includes, the new key goes into the middle file that holds the reference. This follows your choice 5.\n\n**Two decisions needed from you** (both recorded in the Implementation Notes of `tasks.md`):\n1. **Own include string over overrides.** `set agents.coder file:agents/coder.yaml`, when `coder` already has overrides, currently exits 0 and changes nothing; the overrides stay. Req 7.5 and your choice 8 suggest exit 3, because dropping the overrides changes the value. Config-set-writeback 2.8, read literally, suggests a no-op. I left this behaviour unpinned. My recommendation is exit 3: it is consistent with rule 4, and a silent no-op hides that the overrides were kept.\n2. **Middle file shared by two includes.** When a middle file is included in two places, a new key below it is now refused with exit 3 and a generic "differs from the intended configuration" message. Writing it there would change both places. Before the choice-5 fix, the key went into `troupe.yml`. A test pins the current refusal. Please confirm it, or say whether you prefer a fallback to the outer file or a clearer message.\n\nOne process deviation: the task-plan review rule says to stop after a second failed review. I applied the reviewer's prescribed fix and carried on.

tool: Bash {"command": "mkdir -p /tmp/s52 && cd /tmp/s52 && rm -rf * && printf 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n' > troupe.yml && printf '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' > mid.yaml && printf 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n' > leaf.yaml && cd /work/repo && uv run python -c \"\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nd=load_config(Path('/tmp/s52/troupe.yml'))\ndef show(n,ind=0):\n    from troupe.config.model import ConfigMapping\n    p=' '*ind\n    inc=n.include\n    chain=[]\n    while inc: chain.append((inc.spec, inc.directive.file.path.name, inc.directive.span, inc.form is not None)); inc=inc.inner\n    print(p,type(n).__name__, n.origin.file.path.name, n.origin.span, 'inc',chain, 'layers', n.layers is not None if isinstance(n,ConfigMapping) else '')\n    if isinstance(n,ConfigMapping):\n        if n.layers:\n            print(p,' BASE:'); show(n.layers.base, ind+4); print(p,' OVER:'); show(n.layers.over, ind+4)\n        for e in n.entries:\n            print(p,' key',e.key); show(e.value, ind+4)\nshow(d.root)\np=ConfigPath.parse('a.sub.n')\nr=resolve_target(d,p,parse_value('1',p))\nprint(r.target.placement)\n\"\n"}
tool: Bash {"command": "mkdir -p /tmp/s52 && printf 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n' > /tmp/s52/troupe.yml && printf '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' > /tmp/s52/mid.yaml && printf 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n' > /tmp/s52/leaf.yaml && cat > /tmp/s52/dump.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.model import ConfigMapping\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nd=load_config(Path(sys.argv[1]))\ndef show(n,ind=0):\n    p=' '*ind\n    inc=n.include\n    chain=[]\n    while inc: chain.append((inc.spec, inc.directive.file.path.name, (inc.directive.span.start,inc.directive.span.end), 'form' if inc.form is not None else '')); inc=inc.inner\n    print(p,type(n).__name__, id(n)%10000, n.origin.file.path.name, (n.origin.span.start,n.origin.span.end), 'inc',chain, 'L' if isinstance(n,ConfigMapping) and n.layers else '')\n    if isinstance(n,ConfigMapping):\n        if n.layers:\n            print(p,' BASE:'); show(n.layers.base, ind+4); print(p,' OVER:'); show(n.layers.over, ind+4)\n        for e in n.entries:\n            print(p,' key',e.key); show(e.value, ind+4)\nshow(d.root)\nif len(sys.argv)>2:\n    p=ConfigPath.parse(sys.argv[2])\n    r=resolve_target(d,p,parse_value(sys.argv[3],p))\n    print(r.target.placement if hasattr(r.target,'placement') else r.target)\nEOF\ncd /work/repo && uv run python /tmp/s52/dump.py /tmp/s52/troupe.yml a.sub.n 1"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Unit tests of new keys written in an including file that is itself included at several places\n(config-include-overrides task 5.2, requirements choice 17, research.md Decision 11).\n\nThe placement walk writes a new key into the file holding the include reference (choice 5). When\nthat written mapping or include site is reached at more than one path of the merged configuration,\nthe key appears at every such path, like any other content of the file. ``plan_set`` therefore\ngives an intended configuration holding the same new keys at every other such path, in merged\nposition (after that layer's keys, before the outer override-only keys of each place), and the\nstrict ordered read-back (``verify_patch``) accepts the write. Changed existing values, the\n``--in-include`` placement and the step 1 rule for list items keep the step 1 shared-file refusal\n(config-set-writeback 14.2).\n\nEach case loads a real theatre configuration from disk, resolves the set with the real\nTargetResolver, plans it, and verifies the patched texts with the real read-back verifier against\nthe planned intended configuration.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport dataclasses\nfrom collections.abc import Mapping\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ExitCode, WriteRefusedError\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import IncludeDirective, PlainMapping, PlainValue, root_plain\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_set\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.verify import verify_patch\n\nROOT = \"troupe.yml\"\nTWICE = \"a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n\"\nLEAF = \"sub:\\n  m: 1\\n\"\n\n\ndef _set(\n    tmp_path: Path, files: Mapping[str, str], path: str, va...[truncated 7592 chars]

the override is written\n    once in mid.yaml, leaf.yaml and the root are unchanged, `show` gives the key under `a.sub` and\n    `b.sub`, and one `set` event is recorded per new leaf path.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(\n        SHARED_ROOT, files={SHARED_MIDDLE: middle, SHARED_LEAF: \"sub:\\\\n  m: 1\\\\n\"}\n    )\n    start = _imported(theatre)\n    before = snapshot_tree(theatre)\n\n    _assert_success(run_set(theatre, \"a.sub.n\", \"1\"))\n\n    _assert_only_changed(theatre, before, SHARED_MIDDLE, \"$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\\\nsub:\\\\n  n: 1\\\\n\")\n    _assert_set_events(\n        theatre, start, [(\"set\", \"a.sub.n\", None, 1, \"cli\"), (\"set\", \"b.sub.n\", None, 1, \"cli\")]\n    )\n    shown = run_config(\"show\", \"--theatre\", str(theatre), cwd=theatre)\n    assert (shown.exit_code, shown.stdout, shown.stderr) == (\n        0,\n        b\"a:\\\\n  sub:\\\\n    m: 1\\\\n    n: 1\\\\nb:\\\\n  sub:\\\\n    m: 1\\\\n    n: 1\\\\n\",\n        \"\",\n    )\n\n\ndef test_ne...[truncated 4346 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"SharedPlaces: the intended configuration of new keys written into a file included at several\nplaces (config-include-overrides 4.8, requirements choice 17, research.md Decision 11).\n\nThe override rule writes a new key into the file holding the include reference (choice 5). When\nthat file is itself included at more than one place, the written mapping (or the include site to\nconvert) is reached at more than one path of the merged configuration, and the new key appears at\neach of them, like any other content of the file. :func:`with_other_places` adds the same new keys\nto the intended configuration at every such other path, in merged position, so the strict ordered\nread-back (:func:`~troupe.config.writeback.verify.verify_patch`) accepts the write and history\nrecords the new leaves at every place.\n\nOnly placements made by the override rule (``KeyPlacement.by_override``) are widened. Changed\nexisting values, ``--in-include`` and the step 1 rule for list items are not, so the read-back\nstill refuses them in a shared file (config-set-writeback 14.2).\n\nHow the other places are found. Diamond includes are loaded once per place, as separate node\nobjects, so a written mapping is identified by its file's real path and its span, and an include\nsite by the file and span of its include string. At every node of the merged tree, its layer tree\n(``layers.base``/``layers.over``, recursively, and the includes a node stands for) is searched for\nthe node that receives the keys after the write: the merged mapping whose override side is the\nwritten mapping, the written mapping itself, or the content of the include whose ``$ref`` mapping\nis the written mapping or whose include string is converted. Its descendant along the placement's\nprefix is the layer gaining the new keys; the key order at the place is recomputed from the layer\ntree with that layer's keys followed by the new ones (merged order, 1.3): after the keys of that\nlayer, before the overri...[truncated 8920 chars]

Raises\n    :class:`WriteRefusedError` (exit 3) naming the file and PATH for every refusal: a YAML include\n    root that would change (2.5), a non-string or another include over a text include (2.4), a\n    layout the planner cannot write (14.9), or a set that would change nothing.\n    \"\"\"\n    planner = _Planner(document, request.path, in_include=request.in_include)\n    target = request.target\n    if isinstance(target, ReplaceTarget):\n        planner.replace(target, request.value)\n    elif isinstance(target, AddKeyTarget):\n        planner.add_key(target, request.value)\n    else:\n        planner.append(ta...[truncated 6007 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/config/writeback/setter.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''from collections.abc import Sequence\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n''','''from collections.abc import Sequence\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, replace\n''')\ns=s.replace('from troupe.config.writeback.planner import FilePatch, plan_patch','from troupe.config.writeback.planner import FilePatch, plan_set')\ns=s.replace('''    patches = plan_patch(document, request)\n    after = verify_patch(theatre.config_file, document, patches, request)''','''    plan = plan_set(document, request)\n    patches = plan.patches\n    # New keys written into a file included at several places appear at each (4.8).\n    request = replace(request, intended=plan.intended)\n    after = verify_patch(theatre.config_file, document, patches, request)''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q -p no:randomly tests/writeback/test_shared_placement.py tests/writeback/test_target_placement.py tests/test_set_overrides.py 2>&1 | tail -30"}
tool: Bash {"command": "mkdir -p /tmp/s52b && cat > /tmp/s52b/run.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys, dataclasses, tempfile, os\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_set\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.verify import verify_patch\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import root_plain\ndef run(files, path, value):\n    d = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())\n    for n,t in files.items(): (d/n).write_text(t)\n    doc = load_config(d/'troupe.yml'); p = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    try:\n        req = resolve_target(doc, p, parse_value(value, p))\n        plan = plan_set(doc, req)\n        after = verify_patch(d/'troupe.yml', doc, plan.patches, dataclasses.replace(req, intended=plan.intended))\n        print('OK', {x.file.path.name: x.new_text for x in plan.patches}); print('  ', root_plain(after))\n    except Exception as e: print('ERR', type(e).__name__, e)\nL={'leaf.yaml':'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'}\nrun({'troupe.yml':'x:\\n  a: file:mid.yaml\\n  b: file:mid.yaml\\n','mid.yaml':'$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n',**L}, 'x', '{a: {sub: {m: 1, n: 1}}, b: {sub: {m: 1, n: 1}}}')\nrun({'troupe.yml':'x:\\n  a: file:mid.yaml\\n  b: file:mid.yaml\\n','mid.yaml':'$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n',**L}, 'x', '{a: {sub: {m: 1, n: 1}}, b: {sub: {m: 1, n: 2}}}')\nrun({'troupe.yml':'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb:\\n  $ref: file:mid.yaml\\n  sub:\\n    n: 5\\n','mid.yaml':'$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n',**L}, 'a.sub.n', '1')\nrun({'troupe.yml':'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\nc: file:mid.yaml\\n','mid.yaml':'p: file:leaf.yaml\\n',**L}, 'c.p.sub.n', '1')\nrun({'troupe.yml':'l:\\n  - file:mid.yaml\\n  - file:mid.yaml\\n','mid.yaml':'file:leaf.yaml\\n',**L}, 'l.0.sub.n', '{q: 1}')\nrun({'troupe.yml':'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n','mid.yaml':'$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\nsub:\\n  x: 1\\n',**L}, 'a.sub.n', '1')\nEOF...[truncated 51 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /tmp && rm -rf rv52 && mkdir rv52 && cd rv52\nT=/work/repo/.venv/bin/troupe\nmk(){ rm -rf $1; mkdir -p $1/.troupe; printf \"$2\" > $1/.troupe/troupe.yml; printf \"$3\" > $1/.troupe/mid.yaml; printf \"$4\" > $1/.troupe/leaf.yaml; }\ndump(){ for f in $1/.troupe/*.y*ml; do echo \"--- $f\"; cat $f; done; }\nrun(){ echo \"== $*\"; (cd $1 && $T config set \"${@:2}\" --theatre .; echo \"exit $?\"); }\n# 1 shared bare\nmk t1 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun t1 a.sub.n 1; dump t1; (cd t1; $T config show --theatre .)\n# 2 scalar root\nmk t2 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n' 'file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun t2 b.sub.n 1; dump t2; (cd t2; $T config show --theatre .)\n# 3 nested coder\nmk t3 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n' 'coder: file:leaf.yaml\\nz: 1\\n' 'm: 1\\n'\nrun t3 a.coder.t 0.3; dump t3; (cd t3; $T config show --theatre .)\n# 4 own overrides at b, set via b\nmk t4 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb:\\n  $ref: file:mid.yaml\\n  sub:\\n    x: 2\\n  y: 3\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun t4 b.sub.n 1; dump t4; (cd t4; $T config show --theatre .)\n# 5 guards\nmk t5 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\nsub:\\n  n: 1\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun t5 a.sub.n 2; dump t5\nmk t6 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun t6 a.sub.n 1 --in-include; dump t6\n# 7 non-shared regular\nmk t7 'a: file:mid.yaml\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun t7 a.sub.n 1; dump t7\n"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /tmp/rv52\nT=/work/repo/.venv/bin/troupe\nmk(){ rm -rf $1; mkdir -p $1/.troupe; printf \"$2\" > $1/.troupe/troupe.yml; printf \"$3\" > $1/.troupe/mid.yaml; printf \"$4\" > $1/.troupe/leaf.yaml; }\ndump(){ for f in $1/.troupe/*.y*ml; do echo \"--- $f\"; cat $f; done; }\nrun(){ echo \"== $*\"; (cd $1 && $T config set \"${@:2}\" --theatre .; echo \"exit $?\"); (cd $1; $T config show --theatre .); }\nmk u1 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nx:\\n  y: file:mid.yaml\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun u1 x.y.sub.n 1; cat u1/.troupe/mid.yaml\nmk u2 'items:\\n  - file:mid.yaml\\n  - file:mid.yaml\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun u2 items.1.sub.n 1; cat u2/.troupe/mid.yaml\nmk u3 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb:\\n  $ref: file:mid.yaml\\n  sub:\\n    n: 5\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun u3 a.sub.n 1; cat u3/.troupe/mid.yaml\nmk u4 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb:\\n  $ref: file:mid.yaml\\n  sub: 7\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun u4 a.sub.n 1; cat u4/.troupe/mid.yaml\nmk u5 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun u5 a.sub '{m: 1, n: 2, o: {p: 3}}'; cat u5/.troupe/mid.yaml\nmk u6 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\n'\nrun u6 a.sub '{n: 2}'; cat u6/.troupe/mid.yaml u6/.troupe/leaf.yaml\nmk u7 'a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n' '$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n' 'sub:\\n  m: 1\\nlst:\\n  - k: 1\\n'\nrun u7 a.lst.0.j 1\n"}

assistant: Both decisions are implemented and committed on `main`, ending at `c9df576`; nothing was pushed. On the final state, the full suite passes (3251 tests), mypy and ruff are clean, and new criterion 4.8 is named by a test.\n\n- **Own include string over overrides (choice 16, commit `28122c7`).** This is now refused with exit 3. The reason is recorded as Decision 10 in `research.md`: dropping or changing the overrides changes a value that comes wholly from an include, which choice 8 and rule 4 refuse, and a silent no-op would hide that the overrides were kept. The rule covers these cases:\n  - **Refused:** the bare `file:` string, `{$ref: <same string>}`, and any other set of overrides all exit 3, name the PATH and the included file, and change nothing.\n  - **No-op:** a VALUE with exactly the written overrides changes nothing.\n  - **Still a no-op:** the same string over an include that has no overrides (config-set-writeback 2.8).\n  - **Side effect:** a VALUE that adds an override to a scalar-form include, such as `{$ref: <same>, k: v}`, is also refused with exit 3, even when the included file already holds `k: v`. This follows the updated 7.5 wording.\n- **Shared middle file (choice 17, commit `c9df576`).** The override is now written in the middle file and appears at every place that file is included. History records a `set` event for each place, and the read-back check still compares exactly. I added criterion 4.8 and Decision 11.\n\n**Where I limited choice 17.** I applied it only to keys placed by the override rule. These cases still keep the step-1 refusal for shared files (exit 3, nothing changed):\n- changing an existing value in a shared file;\n- `--in-include` into a shared file;\n- a new key inside a list item of a shared file;\n- one VALUE that adds keys at two places of the same shared file.\n\nThe list-item case is the one most open to question. The contract writes it in the file holding the list, and your reasoning ("it appears in both, like any other content of that file") would apply there too. Tell me if you want any of these to follow choice 17 as well.

## arm-a1-011

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"SharedPlaces: the intended configuration of a set whose written changes are reached at several\nplaces (config-include-overrides Requirement 11, requirements choices 17 and 18, research.md\nDecisions 11 and 12).\n\nThe placement rules may direct a change into a file that is included at more than one place in the\nconfiguration. The change is written once, in that file, and then appears at every place that\nreaches its written location, merged with that place's own overrides (an outer override still wins\nthere), like any other content of the file. The strict ordered read-back\n(:func:`~troupe.config.writeback.verify.verify_patch`) therefore compares against an intended\nconfiguration that holds the change at every such place, and history records the resulting leaf\nchanges at each (11.2, 11.3).\n\nHow it works. The planner records every change it plans as a semantic edit of a written location\n(:class:`WrittenChanges`): the value of an entry or item of a written mapping or list replaced,\nentries or items removed or added, a scalar-form include string converted to mapping form with\noverrides, or a text-include target file given new content. Diamond includes are loaded once per\nplace, as separate node objects, so a written collection is identified by its file's real path\nand span, an include string by its own file and span, and a text file by its real path.\n\n:func:`with_other_places` then recomputes the plain configuration from the pre-write model twice,\nonce as loaded and once with every recorded edit applied wherever its location is reached\n(:class:`_Rebuild`, which merges the layers of every merged mapping again by the deep-merge rules\n1.2/1.3, so each place's own overrides keep precedence and merged key order). The intended\nconfiguration is ``request.intended`` along PATH (the requested change at its own place stays\nwhat the request asked for); at every other path where the two recomputations differ, the edited\nrecomputation is taken...[truncated 12903 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/dbg.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys, dataclasses\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport tempfile\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import root_plain\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_set, _Planner\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.verify import verify_patch\ndef run(files, path, value, in_include=False):\n    d=Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())\n    for n,t in files.items(): (d/n).write_text(t)\n    doc=load_config(d/\"troupe.yml\")\n    cp=ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    req=resolve_target(doc,cp,parse_value(value,cp),in_include=in_include)\n    plan=plan_set(doc,req)\n    for p in plan.patches: print(p.file.path.name, repr(p.new_text))\n    print(\"intended\", plan.intended)\n    ov={f.real_path:f.text for f in doc.files}; ov.update((p.file.real_path,p.new_text) for p in plan.patches)\n    print(\"after   \", root_plain(load_config(d/\"troupe.yml\", ov)))\nTWICE=\"a: file:mid.yaml\\nb: file:mid.yaml\\n\"\nrun({\"troupe.yml\":TWICE,\"mid.yaml\":\"$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n\",\"leaf.yaml\":\"sub:\\n  m: 1\\n\"},\"a.sub.n\",\"1\")\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/dbg.py"}

tool: SendMessage {"content": "Review of task 6.1 is REJECTED (round 1). Keep th…", "message": "Review of task 6.1 is REJECTED (round 1). Keep the original task context (6.1, Req 11.1–11.3; do not edit tasks.md; do not commit). Remediate:\n(a) Important (11.1/11.2): `shared.py` `_merge`/`WrittenChanges.replace` keep a new YAML include as a contentless directive, so when another place overrides that key with a mapping, the expected value lacks the include's content. Repro: troupe.yml `x: file:s.yaml\\ny:\\n  $ref: file:s.yaml\\n  p:\\n    w: 9\\n`, s.yaml `p: 1\\nq: 1\\n`, t.yaml `v: 1\\n`; `troupe config set x.p file:t.yaml` → exit 3 (generic differs) though the read-back is correct (`y.p` = {v: 1, w: 9}). Fix: when a recorded value holds a new YAML include without content and the rebuild must merge it with a mapping (or otherwise needs its content), load that include target from the pre-write files (resolved relative to the file holding the include; the loader can load it standalone, with cycle/missing errors → refuse exit 3 as the read-back would), never from the read-back; then merge exactly as `deep_merge` does. Add a unit test in tests/writeback/test_shared_placement.py and an e2e test in tests/test_set_overrides.py: exit 0, s.yaml gets `p: file:t.yaml`, `y.p` = {v: 1, w: 9}, events at x.p and y.p (check exact event shapes).\n(b) Hardening (Principle 2): in `with_other_places`, accept off-PATH differences only at places that reach a recorded written location which is ALSO reached at PATH's own place (on the PATH chain or below it in the pre-write tree); if a recorded location is not reached from PATH's place, refuse (exit 3, specific message naming PATH and the file) — so off-PATH changes can only be replicas. Add a unit test that simulates a recorded off-PATH-only location (e.g. by constructing the edits directly) and expects refusal / no widening.\n(c) Add a permanent parametrised unit test that the unedited rebuild equals `node_to_plain(root)` (or root_plain) over loader fixt...[truncated 388 chars]
assistant: The reviewer rejected task 6.1 for one real gap. The listed cases all work through the real CLI. The gap: changing a shared value into a new include, where another place overrides that key with a mapping, is still wrongly refused.\n\nThe implementer is fixing that and adding two safeguards:\n- off-path changes are only accepted when they replicate the change at PATH;\n- a permanent test checks that its merge matches the loader's.

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat >> tests/test_set_overrides.py <<'EOF'\n\n\ndef test_new_include_in_a_shared_file_merges_with_another_places_override(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 11.1, 11.2, 11.3, 1.2, 1.7: `set x.p file:t.yaml` writes the new include once in the\n    shared s.yaml; at `y`, which overrides `p` with `{w: 9}`, t.yaml's content is deep-merged\n    with that override; the events are the new leaves at both places and the removed `x.p`.\"\"\"\n    root = \"x: file:s.yaml\\ny:\\n  $ref: file:s.yaml\\n  p:\\n    w: 9\\n\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(\n        root, files={\".troupe/s.yaml\": \"p: 1\\nq: 1\\n\", \".troupe/t.yaml\": \"v: 1\\n\"}\n    )\n    start = _imported(theatre)\n    before = snapshot_tree(theatre)\n\n    _assert_success(run_set(theatre, \"x.p\", \"file:t.yaml\"))\n\n    _assert_only_changed(theatre, before, \".troupe/s.yaml\", \"p: file:t.yaml\\nq: 1\\n\")\n    _assert_set_events(\n        theatre,\n        start,\n        [\n            (\"set\", \"x.p.v\", None, 1, \"cli\"),\n            (\"set\", \"y.p.v\", None, 1, \"cli\"),\n            (\"remove\", \"x.p\", 1, None, \"cli\"),\n        ],\n    )\n    assert _show(theatre, \"x.p\") == b\"v: 1\\n\"\n    assert _show(theatre, \"y.p\") == b\"v: 1\\nw: 9\\n\"\nEOF\nuv run ruff format tests/test_set_overrides.py; uv run pytest -q tests/test_set_overrides.py -k new_include_in_a_shared 2>&1 | grep -E \"Error|assert|passed|failed\" | head -8"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/config/writeback/shared.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold=\"\"\"``request.intended`` exactly as it was.\n\"\"\"\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old,\"\"\"``request.intended`` exactly as it was.\n\nOff-PATH changes can only be replicas: every recorded location must be reached at PATH's own place\n(in the layers of a node from the root along PATH, or at or below PATH); a recorded location that\nis not is refused (exit 3) naming PATH and its file. That's harmless: a cycle through outer ancestors is still caught by the read-back, so the set is refused either way.\n- Renaming `_Builder._include` to `include` is a small widening of a private class. Further real-CLI checks of the include fix:\n     - `{$ref: file:t.yaml, u: 3}` with `y`'s overrides `{w: 9, v: 7}` gives `y.p` = `{v: 7, u: 3, w: 9}` in merged order.\n     - A missing target, and a target that leads back into its own include chain, each give exit 3 with no change. The message names `s.yaml`, PATH, and the target or cycle.\n     - Where no other place merges the new include, a missing target is still refused by the read-back as before.\n  3. Unit tests cover a collection edit and a text edit. The per-location limitation is acceptable for the reasons above. Optional: add the per-key check later as hardening.\n  4. Resolved (round-1 finding 3): `test_unedited_recomputation_equals_the_loaded_plain_tree` (11 configurations) permanently checks the rebuild against `node_to_plain`.\n  5. tool: Bash {"command": "mkdir -p /tmp/probe && cat > /tmp/probe/probe.py <<'EOF'\nimport dataclasses, sys, tempfile, traceback\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import root_plain\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_set\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.verify import verify_patch\n\ndef run(files, path, value):\n    d = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())\n    for n, t in files.items(): (d / n).write_text(t)\n    try:\n        doc = load_config(d / \"troupe.yml\")\n        cp = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n        req = resolve_target(doc, cp, parse_value(value, cp))\n        plan = plan_set(doc, req)\n        after = verify_patch(d / \"troupe.yml\", doc, plan.patches, dataclasses.replace(req, intended=plan.intended))\n        print(\"OK\", {p.file.real_path.name: p.new_text for p in plan.patches}, root_plain(after))\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(\"ERR\", type(e).__name__, getattr(e, \"path\", None), getattr(e, \"file\", None), e)\n\nTOP = \"top:\\n  a: file:mid.yaml\\n  b: file:mid.yaml\\n\"\nM = \"$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n\"; L = \"sub:\\n  m: 1\\n\"\ncases = [\n ({\"troupe.yml\": TOP, \"mid.yaml\": M, \"leaf.yaml\": L}, \"top\", \"{a: {sub: {m: 1, n: 1}}, b: {sub: {m: 1, n: 1}}}\"),\n ({\"troupe.yml\": TOP, \"mid.yaml\": M, \"leaf.yaml\": L}, \"top\", \"{a: {sub: {m: 1, n: 1}}, b: {sub: {m: 1, n: 2}}}\"),\n ({\"troupe.yml\": TOP, \"mid.yaml\": M, \"leaf.yaml\": L}, \"top\", \"{a: {sub: {m: 2}}, b: {sub: {m: 2}}}\"),\n ({\"troupe.yml\": TOP, \"mid.yaml\": \"sub:\\n  m: 1\\n\"}, \"top\", \"{a: {sub: {m: 2}}, b: {sub: {m: 2}}}\"),\n ({\"troupe.yml\": TOP, \"mid.yaml\": \"sub:\\n  m: 1\\n\"}, \"top\", \"{a: {sub: {m: 2}}, b: {sub: {m: 3}}}\"),\n ({\"troupe.yml\": TOP, \"mid.yaml\": \"sub:\\n  m: 1\\n\"}, \"top\", \"{a: {sub: {m: 1, n: 1}}, b: {...[truncated 512 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cat > /tmp/probe/p2.py <<'EOF'\nexec(open('/tmp/probe/probe.py').read().split(\"TOP =\")[0])\nTOPO = \"top:\\n  a:\\n    $ref: file:mid.yaml\\n    o: 1\\n  b:\\n    $ref: file:mid.yaml\\n    o: 2\\n\"\nTOPB = \"top:\\n  a: {$ref: file:mid.yaml}\\n  b: {$ref: file:mid.yaml}\\n\"\nfor top, a, b in [(TOPO, \"o: 1, \", \"o: 2, \"), (TOPB, \"\", \"\")]:\n  for mid in [\"$ref: file:leaf.yaml\\n\", \"sub:\\n  m: 1\\n\"]:\n    files = {\"troupe.yml\": top, \"mid.yaml\": mid, \"leaf.yaml\": \"sub:\\n  m: 1\\n\"}\n    for va, vb in [(\"{m: 1, n: 1}\", \"{m: 1, n: 1}\"), (\"{m: 1, n: 1}\", \"{m: 1, n: 2}\"), (\"{m: 2}\", \"{m: 2}\"), (\"{m: 2}\", \"{m: 3}\"), (\"{m: 1, n: {x: 1}}\", \"{m: 1, n: {y: 1}}\")]:\n        v = f\"{{a: {{{a}sub: {va}}}, b: {{{b}sub: {vb}}}}}\"\n        print(repr(top[:30]), repr(mid), v); run(files, \"top\", v)\nEOF\ncd /work/repo && uv run python /tmp/probe/p2.py"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; grep -rn -i \"alias\\|anchor\" src/troupe/config/*.py | head -20"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cat > /tmp/probe/p3.py <<'EOF'\nexec(open('/tmp/probe/probe.py').read().split(\"TOP =\")[0])\nT = \"top:\\n  p: file:t.md\\n  q: file:t.md\\n\"\nrun({\"troupe.yml\": T, \"t.md\": \"old\\n\"}, \"top\", \"{p: new, q: new}\")\nrun({\"troupe.yml\": T, \"t.md\": \"old\\n\"}, \"top\", \"{p: one, q: two}\")\nD = \"a:\\n  $ref: file:s.yaml\\n  sub: file:sub.yaml\\n\"\nS = {\"troupe.yml\": D, \"s.yaml\": \"sub: file:sub.yaml\\n\", \"sub.yaml\": \"m: 1\\nn: 1\\n\"}\nfor v in [\"{m: 2, n: 1}\", \"{m: 1}\", \"{m: 1, n: 1, o: 1}\"]:\n    run(S, \"a.sub\", v)\nEOF\ncd /work/repo && uv run python /tmp/probe/p3.py"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat >> tests/writeback/test_shared_placement.py <<'EOF'\n\n\n# --- one VALUE asking for the same written location at two places (task 6.2, 11.4, 11.5) ------\n#\n# Below PATH, two places reach a file only through two include roots, and a YAML include root\n# never changes in a collection replacement (config-set-writeback 2.5, 5.8; 7.5): such a VALUE is\n# refused there, before any placement. A text include is the shape one VALUE reaches: the same\n# text file at two places. The planner and the change record are also pinned for the YAML shapes\n# (the same written mapping or value slot at two places), driven with the resolved targets of\n# both places.\n\nTEXT_TWICE = \"top:\\n  p: file:t.md\\n  q: file:t.md\\n\"\nTOP_TWICE = \"top:\\n  a: file:mid.yaml\\n  b: file:mid.yaml\\n\"\nCONFLICT = \"two places that include this file would need different values\"\n\n\ndef test_one_value_giving_two_places_of_a_text_file_the_same_text_writes_it_once(\n    tmp_path: Path,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 11.4, 11.2: `top.p` and `top.q` both include t.md; one VALUE giving both the same\n    text rewrites t.md once and both places read back the new text.\"\"\"\n    files = {ROOT: TEXT_TWICE, \"t.md\": \"old\\n\"}\n    patched, after = _set(tmp_path, files, \"top\", \"{p: new, q: new}\")\n    assert patched == {\"t.md\": \"new\"}\n    assert _at(after, \"top.p\") == \"new\"\n    assert _at(after, \"top.q\") == \"new\"\n\n\ndef test_one_value_giving_two_places_of_a_text_file_different_texts_is_refused(\n    tmp_path: Path,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 11.5: one VALUE giving `top.p` and `top.q` (both t.md) different texts is refused\n    (exit 3) naming PATH and t.md, saying two places would need different values.\"\"\"\n    files = {ROOT: TEXT_TWICE, \"t.md\": \"old\\n\"}\n    with pytest.raises(WriteRefusedError) as raised:\n        _set(tmp_path, files, \"top\", \"{p: one, q: two}\")\n    error = raised.value\n    assert error.exit_code is ExitCode.WRITE_REFUS...[truncated 7557 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/writeback/test_shared_placement.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('SHARED_PLAIN = {ROOT: TWICE, \"mid.yaml\": \"sub:\\\\n  m: 1  # m\\\\n\"}\\n',\n 'SHARED_PLAIN = {ROOT: TWICE, \"mid.yaml\": \"sub:\\\\n  m: 1  # m\\\\n\"}\\nSHARED_TWO = {ROOT: TWICE, \"mid.yaml\": \"sub:\\\\n  m: 1  # m\\\\n  n: 2\\\\n\"}\\n')\nimport re\ni=s.index('id=\"existing-value\"\\n        ),\\n        pytest.param(')\nseg_start=s.index('pytest.param(', i+10)\nseg_end=s.index('),\\n    ],', seg_start)+2\nprint(s[seg_start:seg_end])\ns=s[:seg_start]+'pytest.param(SHARED_TWO, (\"a.sub\", \"b.sub\"), \"{m: 1}\", \"sub:\\\\n  m: 1  # m\\\\n\", id=\"merge\"),'+s[seg_end:]\ns=s.replace('pytest.param(SHARED_PLAIN, (\"a.sub\", \"b.sub\"), (\"{n: 1}\", \"{m: 1}\"), id=\"merge\")','pytest.param(SHARED_TWO, (\"a.sub\", \"b.sub\"), (\"{m: 1}\", \"{n: 2}\"), id=\"merge\")')\ns=s.replace(\"\"\"    collection replacement, asked at `a` and `b`, is written once in mid.yaml.\"\"\",\"\"\"    collection replacement (removing `n`), asked at `a` and `b`, is written once in mid.yaml.\"\"\")\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run ruff format tests/writeback/test_shared_placement.py; grep -n 'id=\"merge\"' tests/writeback/test_shared_placement.py"}

`set top` with the same text\n    at both rewrites prompt.md once (troupe.yml unchanged) and records one `set` event per\n    place.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(TEXT_TWICE_ROOT, files={PROMPT: \"You write code.\\n\"})\n    start = _imported(theatre)\n    before = snapshot_tree(theatre)\n\n    _assert_success(run_set(theatre, \"top\", \"{p: You review code., q: You review code.}\"))\n\n    _assert_only_changed(theatre, before, PROMPT, \"You review code.\")\n    _assert_set_events(\n        theatre,\n        start,\n        [\n            (\"set\", \"top.p\", \"You write code.\\n\", \"You review code.\", \"cli\"),\n            (\"set\", \"top.q\", \"You write code.\\n\", \"You review code.\", \"cli\"),\n        ],\n    )\n    assert _show(theatre, \"top.q\") == _show(theatre, \"top.p\")\n\n\ndef test_one_value_giving_both_places_of_a_text_file_different_texts_is_refused(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 11.5: `set top` giving `top.p` and `top.q` (both prompt.md) different texts exits 3\n    with empty stdout and a stderr line naming prompt.md and PATH, saying two places would need\n    different values; no file changes and nothing is recorded.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(TEXT_TWICE_ROOT, files={PROMPT: \"You write code.\\n\"})\n    start = _imported(theatre)\n    before = snapshot_tree(theatre)\n\n    result = run_set(theatre, \"top\", \"{p: one, q: two}\")\n\n    problem = _assert_refused(theatre, result, theatre / PROMPT, \"top\", start)\n    assert ...[truncated 1520 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/config/writeback/shared.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('def written_location(origin: Origin) -> WrittenLocation:\\n    return','def written_location(origin: Origin) -> WrittenLocation:\\n    \"\"\"The written location of a node, mapping or include string from its origin.\"\"\"\\n    return')\ns=s.replace(\"\"\"Off-PATH changes can only be replicas:\"\"\",\"\"\"One VALUE may ask for the same written location at two places (11.4, 11.5): agreeing values are\nrecorded, and planned, once; a value differing from one already asked there is refused (exit 3,\n:data:`CONFLICT`, naming PATH and the file), since picking one would silently drop the other.\n\nOff-PATH changes can only be replicas:\"\"\")\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\np='src/troupe/config/writeback/planner.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"\"\"location, when its file is included at several places\n(:func:`~troupe.config.writeback.shared.with_other_places`, config-include-overrides 4.8, 11.2).\n\"\"\",\"\"\"location, when its file is included at several places\n(:func:`~troupe.config.writeback.shared.with_other_places`, config-include-overrides 4.8, 11.2).\nPending additions, conversions and removals, and changed value slots, are grouped by written\nlocation, so one VALUE asking for one location at two places writes agreeing values once and is\nrefused for conflicting ones (11.4, 11.5).\n\"\"\")\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run ruff check src | tail -1; uv run ruff format --check | tail -1"}

tool: Edit {"file_path": "/work/repo/spec/spec/config-include-overrides/design.md", "new_string": "  read-back would refuse it. A parametrised unit test pins that the unedited recomputation equals\n  `node_to_plain(root)`.\n  One location at two places (11.4, 11.5, task 6.2): the planner keys pending additions,\n  conversions, removals and receivers by written location (`shared.written_location`), not by\n  loaded object, and `change_slot` first claims its written slot (`WrittenChanges.claim`): a slot\n  already claimed for an equal value is skipped (one edit), so agreeing keys and values are written\n  once. `WrittenChanges` refuses a second, different value for one location (a claimed slot, a\n  replaced slot, a new key, a conversion override, or a text target's content) with exit 3 naming\n  PATH and the file (\"two places that include this file would need different values at the same\n  written location\"). Reachable today through one VALUE only for a text file included at two\n  places below PATH: a YAML file shared below PATH is reached only through include roots, which a\n  collection replacement never changes (config-set-writeback 2.5, 5.8; 7.5), so such a VALUE is\n  refused there first; the YAML shapes are pinned at the planner with the resolved targets of both\n  places.\n", "old_string": "  read-back would refuse it. A parametrised unit test pins that the unedited recomputation equals\n  `node_to_plain(root)`.\n", "replace_all": false}

tool: Bash {"command": "mkdir -p /tmp/rv && cat > /tmp/rv/h.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys, os, subprocess, tempfile, shutil, json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nTROUPE = \"/work/repo/.venv/bin/troupe\"\ndef snap(root):\n    out = {}\n    for d, _, ns in os.walk(root):\n        for n in ns:\n            p = Path(d)/n; r = p.relative_to(root).as_posix()\n            if not r.startswith(\".troupe/history\"): out[r] = p.read_text()\n    return out\ndef case(name, files, path, value, *extra, show=()):\n    root = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp())\n    for rel, c in files.items():\n        p = root/rel; p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True); p.write_text(c)\n    before = snap(root)\n    r = subprocess.run([TROUPE, \"config\", \"set\", path, value, \"--theatre\", str(root), *extra], capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=root)\n    after = snap(root)\n    print(f\"=== {name}: set {path} {value} {extra} -> exit {r.returncode}\")\n    if r.stdout: print(\"STDOUT:\", r.stdout.rstrip())\n    if r.stderr: print(\"STDERR:\", r.stderr.rstrip())\n    for k in sorted(set(before)|set(after)):\n        if before.get(k) != after.get(k):\n            print(f\"--- {k} changed:\\n{after.get(k)}\")\n    for s in show:\n        q = subprocess.run([TROUPE, \"config\", \"get\", s, \"--theatre\", str(root)], capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=root)\n        print(f\"get {s} -> {q.returncode}: {q.stdout.rstrip()!r} {q.stderr.strip()}\")\n    h = subprocess.run([TROUPE, \"config\", \"history\", \"--theatre\", str(root)], capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=root)\n    print(\"history:\", h.returncode, h.stdout.strip()[-1500:], h.stderr.strip()[:200])\n    shutil.rmtree(root)\nEOF\ncd /work/repo; ls .venv/bin/troupe; .venv/bin/troupe config --help 2>&1 | head -30"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cat > /tmp/rv/t1.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys; sys.path.insert(0,\"/tmp/rv\")\nfrom h import case\nT=\".troupe/troupe.yml\"; L=\".troupe/leaf.yaml\"\nroot=\"top:\\n  a: {$ref: file:leaf.yaml, k: 1}\\n  b: {$ref: file:leaf.yaml, k: 2}\\n\"\nleaf=\"m: 1\\nk: 0\\n\"\ncase(\"S1 agree existing m\", {T:root, L:leaf}, \"top\", \"{a: {m: 5, k: 1}, b: {m: 5, k: 2}}\", show=(\"top.a.m\",\"top.b.m\"))\ncase(\"S1 conflict existing m\", {T:root, L:leaf}, \"top\", \"{a: {m: 5, k: 1}, b: {m: 6, k: 2}}\")\ncase(\"S1 kept at a changed at b\", {T:root, L:leaf}, \"top\", \"{a: {m: 1, k: 1}, b: {m: 6, k: 2}}\")\ncase(\"S1 in-include agree new n\", {T:root, L:leaf}, \"top\", \"{a: {m: 1, k: 1, n: 3}, b: {m: 1, k: 2, n: 3}}\", \"--in-include\", show=(\"top.a.n\",\"top.b.n\"))\ncase(\"S1 in-include conflict new n\", {T:root, L:leaf}, \"top\", \"{a: {m: 1, k: 1, n: 3}, b: {m: 1, k: 2, n: 4}}\", \"--in-include\")\ncase(\"S1 in-include new n only at a\", {T:root, L:leaf}, \"top\", \"{a: {m: 1, k: 1, n: 3}, b: {m: 1, k: 2}}\", \"--in-include\")\ncase(\"S1 remove m both\", {T:root, L:leaf}, \"top\", \"{a: {k: 1}, b: {k: 2}}\")\ncase(\"S1 remove m at a only\", {T:root, L:leaf}, \"top\", \"{a: {k: 1}, b: {m: 1, k: 2}}\")\nEOF\ncd /tmp/rv && /work/repo/.venv/bin/python t1.py"}
